Homeomorphic modified wave operators for the Vlasov–Poisson system
Léo Bigorgne

Abstract
We prove modified scattering for small data solutions to the Vlasov–Poisson system in a functional framework where the initial data, scattering states, and asymptotic convergence are measured in the same topology. In addition, we show that the corresponding wave operators define homeomorphisms between the spaces of initial and scattering data, while enjoying a local Lipschitz continuity property in weaker norms. As a consequence, in the repulsive case, large spherically symmetric solutions are asymptotically stable. The proof relies in particular on the introduction of a suitable system of dynamic coordinates adapted to the asymptotic nonlinear flow.



1
Introduction

In this paper, we investigate the dynamics of solutions to the Vlasov–Poisson system, as well as the continuity properties of the associated scattering operators. The equations read

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi[f]\cdot\nabla_{v}f=0,\\
\Delta_{x}\phi[f]=\rho[f],\end{cases}

(VP)

where

•

the system describes the evolution, in phase space
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, of a large number of particles of mass
m=1
. The interaction between the particles is attractive when
\lambda=1
and repulsive when
\lambda=-1
.

•

The distribution function
f\colon\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
gives the density of particles in phase space at time
t
, position
x
and velocity
v
. Although physically nonnegative,
f
is allowed to take negative values in this paper. The spatial density
\rho[f]
is given by

\rho[f](t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v.

•

The function
\phi[f]\colon\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}
is the potential of the force field
-\nabla_{x}\phi[f]
, which is self-consistently generated by the particles through the Poisson equation.

The solutions to (
VP
) are global in time for a broad class of initial data
[
26
,
20
]
. Outside a few specific cases, such as the small data regime
[
2
,
18
,
31
,
32
,
11
,
29
]
, the spherically symmetric setting
[
15
,
21
]
, and perturbations thereof
[
28
]
, little is known about the asymptotic behavior of solutions.

1.1  Modified scattering dynamics for small data

It was shown by Ionescu-Pausader-Wang-Widmayer
[
19
]
that small data solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system satisfy modified scattering. There exists asymptotic states
f_{\pm\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}f\big(t,x+tv\pm\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\pm\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)=f_{\pm\infty}(x,v),\qquad\qquad\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[f_{\pm\infty}\big]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}f_{\pm\infty}(x,\cdot)\mathrm{d}x.

(1)

Contrary to the high-dimensional case
[
23
]
, because of long-range effects of the force field, the nonlinear characteristics cannot be asymptotically approximated by a linear one. Indeed, restricting to positive times, one can show, as predicted by the linearised system, that the force field is asymptotically self-similar

\lim_{t\to+\infty}t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)=\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{+\infty}](v).

At this stage, however, one cannot yet assert that the limiting profile is
\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{+\infty}]
. One therefore expects the characteristics
t\mapsto(X,V)(t)
of (
VP
) to satisfy
V(t)\to v_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, as
t\to+\infty
, and

V(t)\approx v_{\infty}+\frac{\lambda}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[f_{+\infty}\big](v_{\infty}),\qquad\qquad X(t)\approx x_{\infty}+tv_{\infty}+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{+\infty}](v_{\infty})\log(t),

which suggests that (
1
) holds. We also refer to
[
10
,
22
]
for related results, and to
[
8
,
30
]
where the asymptotic expansions of
\nabla_{x}\phi[f]
and
\rho[f]
are shown to be polyhomogeneous.

In
[
13
]
, the converse problem to (
1
) has been addressed. Any sufficiently regular scattering state
f_{+\infty}
is attained by a unique solution
f
to (
VP
) as in (
1
). If
f_{+\infty}
is small enough,
f
is in addition defined for all
t\geq 0
. By exploiting the time invariance of the system, this allows to define the small data forward and backward (modified) wave operators
\mathscr{W}_{+}
and
\mathscr{W}_{-}
, as well as the (modified) scattering map
\mathscr{S}
,

\mathscr{W}_{-}\colon f_{-\infty}\mapsto f_{0},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathscr{W}_{+}\colon f_{+\infty}\mapsto f_{0},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathscr{S}=\mathscr{W}_{+}^{-1}\circ\mathscr{W}_{-}\colon f_{-\infty}\mapsto f_{+\infty}.

The main two goals of this paper are the following.

1.

Find topologies which make the scattering operators
\mathscr{W}_{+}
,
\mathscr{W}_{-}
, and
\mathscr{S}
, endomorphisms. This was listed as an open problem in
[
13
]
. Typycally, one assumes that the initial data
f_{0}
belongs to a weighted Sobolev space, proves (
1
) in a weaker topology, and obtains a scattering state
f_{+\infty}
lying in a weighted Sobolev space with reduced regularity (both in terms of derivatives and weights). A similar loss occurs in the construction of
\mathscr{W}_{+}
.

In
[
13
]
, the construction of
\mathscr{S}
requires weighted
W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
regularity for
f_{-\infty}
whereas the resulting state
f_{+\infty}
is only controlled in the unweighted space
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

2.

We investigate the regularity properties of the scattering operators. In particular, we prove that they are homeomorphisms in the natural norm of the problem, and locally Lipschitz in a weaker norm. This requires, in particular, to show the asymptotic stability of solutions satisfying modified scattering.

In the repulsive case
\lambda=-1
, this improves upon the result of Schaeffer
[
28
]
, who showed that perturbations of spherically symmetric solutions exist globally and decay, but without establishing asymptotic stability.

Let us also mention that modified scattering occurs in the following settings.

•

For perturbations of a point charge for the repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system
[
25
]
.

•

For small data solutions to the two-dimensional Vlasov-Poisson system with the external unstable trapping potential
-|x|^{2}/2
(see
[
7
,
17
]
).

•

For the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Riesz system, where the Newtonian kernel
|x|^{-1}
appearing in (
VP
) is replaced by
|x|^{-\alpha}
, with
0<\alpha<1
. The case
1-\delta<\alpha<1
, for sufficiently small
\delta>0
, was addressed in
[
16
]
, and
[
14
]
extended the result to the range
1/2<\alpha<1
.

Finally, for the Vlasov-Maxwell system in the small data regime, the distribution function exhibits a modified scattering dynamic
[
6
,
24
,
9
]
, whereas the Maxwell field undergoes linear scattering, with a nontrivial electromagnetic memory effect
[
5
]
.

1.2  Statement of the main results

We start by introducing the energy norms considered throughout this paper. We fix a Lebesgue exponent
3/2<p<\infty
, and we define
p_{\infty}\coloneqq\lfloor 3/p\rfloor+1
, so that the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
holds.

Definition 1.1
.

Let
N\geq 0
,
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
g\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
. Then, we define

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g]\coloneqq\sum_{|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g(x,v)\Big|^{p}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Moreover,

•

If
g
does depend on the time variable, we will often write
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)
for
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)]
,

•

We denote by
\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}\subset W^{N,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\big(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)
the Banach space composed by the functions
g
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g]<+\infty
.

•

If
n=(7,7)
, we will simply write
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]
for
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g]
and
\mathcal{B}_{N}
for
\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}
.

Remark 1.2
.

We work in the range
1<p<\infty
to have access to elliptic regularity (see also Remark
1.8
below). The condition
p>3/2
implies that the first order derivatives of the Newtonian kernel
|x|^{-1}
are
L^{p}
-integrable at infinity. The last assumption could be relaxed, at the expense of additional technicalities in the proof.

We now state our main result concerning the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
and
f_{\mathrm{data}}\in\mathcal{B}_{N}
. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}[N,p]>0
such that, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\mathrm{data}}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then

(A)

Global existence.
The unique classical solution
f
to (
VP
), such that
f(0,\cdot,\cdot)=f_{\mathrm{data}}
, is global in time.

(B)

Boundedness.
There exists an asymptotic state
f_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}_{N}
such that the function

g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)

(2)

is uniformly bounded,

\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\quad\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\mathrm{data}}].

(C)

Modified scattering in the strong norm.
We have
\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\to 0
as
t\to+\infty
, and

\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\mathrm{data}}].

Conversely, given
f_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}_{N}
, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}[N,p]>0
such that, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, then

•

There exists a unique global classical solution
f
to (
VP
) such that
g(t,\cdot,\cdot)\to f_{\infty}
as
t\to+\infty
.

•

The properties
(B)
–
(C)
hold with
f_{\mathrm{data}}
replaced by the asymptotic data
f_{\infty}
.

Remark 1.4
.

If, in addition,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(n_{x},n_{v})}[f_{\mathrm{data}}]<+\infty
for some
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
, then there exists
\mathbf{D}[N,n,p]>0
such that

\forall t\geq 0,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(n_{x},n_{v})}[g](t)\leq\mathbf{D}\,\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(n_{x},n_{v})}[f_{\mathrm{data}}].

Moreover, the convergences in
(C)
also hold in the stronger norms
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[\cdot]
and
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
. We note that the convergence rate could be improved at the expense of requiring one additional
\langle v\rangle
-weight in the norm on the right-hand side.

We are then able to define the (modified) wave operators.

Definition 1.5
.

Let
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
, and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{\pm}^{N,n}\subset\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}
be the largest set such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\pm}:\mathcal{O}_{\pm}^{N,n}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}

\displaystyle f_{\pm\infty}

\displaystyle\mapsto f(0,\cdot,\cdot),

where
f
is the unique solution to (
VP
) which satisfies modified scattering toward
f_{\pm\infty}
, is well-defined. The maps
\mathscr{W}_{+}
and
\mathscr{W}_{-}
are the modified wave operators associated to
+\infty
and
-\infty
, respectively, for the Vlasov-Poisson system.

We now state our main results concerning the regularity of the scattering operators.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
and
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
. Then,

•

the set
\mathcal{O}_{\pm}^{N,n}
is non-empty and open,

•

\mathscr{W}_{\pm}
is a homeomorphism from
\mathcal{O}_{\pm}^{N,n}
onto its image, with respect to the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
,

•

\mathscr{W}_{\pm}
and
\mathscr{W}_{\pm}^{-1}
are locally Lipschitz with respect to the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[\cdot]
,

•

in the case
\lambda=-1
,
\mathcal{O}_{\pm}^{N,n}
contains a neighborhood of any spherically symmetric
f_{\infty}\in C^{N}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

It allows to deduce the next result for the scattering map
\mathscr{S}\colon f_{-\infty}\mapsto f_{+\infty}
.

Corollary 1.7
.

Let
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
and
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
. The scattering map
\mathscr{S}\coloneqq\mathscr{W}_{+}^{-1}\circ\mathscr{W}_{-}
is a homeomorphism from
\mathcal{O}_{-}^{N,n}
onto its image, with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
, and is locally Lipschitz with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[\cdot]
.

1.3  Strategy of the proof

1.3.1 Small data solutions

We first discuss the difficulties arising in the proof of the forward evolution part of Theorem
1.3
. One way for proving (
1
) consists in writing, using (
VP
), that the function
g
in (
2
) satisfies

\partial_{t}g(t,x,v)=\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle\big)-\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}g+\frac{b(t,x,v)}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{x,v}g(t,x,v),

where
|b(t,x,v)|\lesssim\log\langle t+1\rangle
, and
|\nabla_{x,v}g|
is uniformly bounded in time. The limiting force field is usually identified before
f_{\infty}
, and satisfies
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)\to\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)
as
t\to+\infty
. The mean value theorem then yields

|\partial_{t}g(t,x,v)|\lesssim\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle x\rangle|\nabla_{x}g|(t,x,v)+|\nabla_{v}g|(t,x,v)\Big).

(3)

A similar estimate holds for the derivatives of
g
. For our purposes, namely proving modified scattering in a functional framework where the initial data, the scattering states, and the asymptotic convergence are measured in the same norm, we identify two difficulties.

1.

There is a
\langle x\rangle
loss in (
3
). Consequently, proving the convergence of
g
requires assuming that
\nabla_{x}g
is bounded in a stronger weighted norm.

2.

There is a loss of regularity in (
3
). Proving the convergence of
g
requires sufficiently good control of
\nabla_{x}g
and
\nabla_{v}g
. Consequently, this approach does not allow one to determine whether top order derivatives of
g
converge. In fact, even proving boundedness for the top order
v
-derivatives of
g
is not straightforward. These derivatives are usually estimated in terms of top order derivatives of
f(t,x+tv,v)
, which grow logarithmically.

We deal with the first problem by propagating hierarchised weighted norms of
g
. For simplicity, we discuss this issue using the profile
f_{\circ}(t,x,v)=f(t,x+tv,v)
instead of
g
since the analysis is similar but technically simpler. At first glance, one may try to consider the
L^{p}
-norms of
\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}
. However, denoting by
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}
the Vlasov operator associated with (
VP
) in the coordinate system
(t,x+tv,v)
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}\big(\partial_{x^{i}}f_{\circ}\big)=-\lambda t\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi[f](t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\circ}+\lambda\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi[f](t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{\circ}.

(4)

In order to propagate such norms, the second term on the right hand side forces us to bound

\langle x\rangle\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,x+tv)\big|\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle}{\langle t+|x+tv|\rangle^{3}}

(5)

by a quantity that decays sufficiently fast in time, uniformly in
(x,v)
. When
|x|\gg t
and
|x+tv|\ll|x|
, this is unfortunately not possible. One way to circumvent this difficulty is to use the inequality
|x|\leq|x+tv|+t|v|
, and to absorb the
|v|
-weight by working instead with
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\circ}]
, namely the
L^{p}
-norms of
\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}
.

The second difficulty can be decomposed into two different problems. Assuming
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f](0)\leq\varepsilon
,

•

Can we prove that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
?

•

Can we show that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}]\to 0
, as
t\to+\infty
?

Since (
3
) allows to prove modified scattering in a weaker norm, we would adress the first problem by Banach-Alaoglu theorem if we could prove that
t\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)
is uniformly bounded. In order to avoid the loss of regularity arising from (
3
), one could be tempted to consider
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}
, the Vlasov operator in the coordinate system
(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
, and to commute it
N
times. However, one of the error term would carry the factor

\nabla_{v}^{2}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v),\qquad\qquad|\gamma|=N,

(6)

that we do not control since we already face a loss of regularity in the convergence
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)\to\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)
. Moreover, even if the elliptic regularity of the Poisson equation provides an estimate of

t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f](t,tv),\qquad\qquad|\gamma|=N,

(7)

the usual approaches allow for a small growth in time of the top order derivatives, preventing us to control (
6
) by an application of the Banach-Alaoglu theorem. We deal with this issue by working in a
dynamical system of coordinates
in which the distribution function admits a limit. In contrast, the previous system of coordinates, associated to
g
, is teleological as it depends on the asymptotic dynamics of the solution. We consider

h(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big),\qquad\qquad\Phi_{f}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv),

(8)

so that we also have
h(t,x,v)\to f_{\infty}(x,v)
as
t\to+\infty
. Moreover, although estimating top order derivatives of
h
also requires controlling (
7
), the use of this dynamical coordinate system allows us to prove that both
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h]
and

\|t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f](t,\cdot)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}
, for
|\gamma|\leq N
, are uniformly bounded in time. This yields
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
.

Remark 1.8
.

Note that controlling the top order derivatives of
f_{\circ}
through (
4
) only requires controlling
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f]
, for
|\gamma|\leq N
. Obtaining improved asymptotics by using a better coordinate system comes at the cost of controlling one additional derivative of the force field. The crucial use of elliptic estimates at top order then requires us to work with
L^{p}
-norms, with
1<p<\infty
. We in fact work in the range
3/2<p<\infty
to avoid technical difficulties. As suggested by (
5
), we will have to exploit the full spatial decay of the force field. However, for
p\leq 3/2
,
\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,\cdot)\notin L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
.

Remark 1.9
.

The
v
-derivatives of
h
, when expressed in terms of derivatives of
f
, are reminiscent of the modified vector fields introduced by Smulevici
[
31
]
(see also
[
12
,
3
,
27
]
for applications to other Vlasov systems). The dynamical coordinate system used in this article seems to capture the advantages of both the modified vector fields introduced in
[
31
]
and the coordinate system
(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
, without suffering from their drawbacks.

The second problem, namely the convergence of
g
with respect to the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
, requires a method avoiding the loss of regularity in (
3
). To this end, we exploit that the transport dynamics singles out a preferred time direction along which no derivative loss occurs. More precisely, we use Duhamel’s principle to obtain

\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}g(t,x,v)=\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}g\big(0,\varphi_{0,t}(x,v)\big)+\int_{s=0}^{t}\Big[\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}\big(\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}g\big)\Big]\big(s,\varphi_{s,t}(x,v)\big)\mathrm{d}s,

where
\varphi_{s,t}(x,v)
denotes the flow associated with
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}
. Using the information obtained previously, one can then show that the second term on the right hand side converges in a weighted
L^{p}_{x,v}
-norm as
t\to+\infty
, at a quantitative rate. The convergence of the first term, without assuming additional regularity on the initial data, is proved by an approximation argument together with the convergence of the transport flow to a Lipschitz volume-preserving homeomorphism.

The backward problem can be handled similarly and is simpler in one respect. Indeed, knowing the scattering data allows one to control (
6
) directly, and therefore to work immediately with the function
g
.

1.3.2 Regularity of the scattering operators

By time-reversibility of the Vlasov–Poisson system, it suffices to focus on
\mathscr{W}_{+}
. The first step of the proof consists in showing that the modified wave operator defines a map between two open sets. In other words, we need to prove that any solution
\mathbf{f}
to (
VP
) satisfying modified scattering is asymptotically stable under perturbations of both the initial and scattering data. Let us now describe the additional difficulties arising in the analysis of the perturbed solution
f
, compared to the small data regime.

•

The error terms in the commuted equations for the derivatives of
f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}
are merely linear in the small quantities. Since these error terms exhibit borderline decay, a Grönwall argument would yield a growth of the form
t^{A}
, with
A
large, for the derivatives of
f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}
, preventing us from closing the bootstrap argument. As a consequence, we are forced to use
dynamical coordinate systems
in order to prove global existence, whereas for small data solutions they are only truly needed for the scattering analysis. We then study
h-\mathbf{h}
, where
h
is given by (
8
) and

\mathbf{h}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathbf{f}\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big),\qquad\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,tv).

•

Derivatives of
h-\mathbf{h}
are coupled to higher-order derivatives of
\mathbf{h}
. As a consequence, the top order derivatives of
h
must be controlled without directly exploiting a small parameter. Moreover, deriving optimal decay estimates for
\nabla_{x}\phi[f]
and its derivatives in terms of
h
leads to a strongly nonlinear dependence on
\mathbb{E}_{N}[h]
.

•

Denoting by
\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}]
a suitable
N-1
-th order norm of
\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
, we will show that

\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}](t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h-\mathbf{h}](t)+\langle t\rangle^{-1/2}\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}](t).

As a consequence, the potentially large implicit constant in
\lesssim
makes the boundedness of
\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
nontrivial, and the estimates must therefore be arranged so as to avoid any circular dependence.

To deal with these difficulties, we proceed as follows.

•

We first appeal to Cauchy stability to deal with a compact interval of time
[0,T_{0}]
. If
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}](0)
is small enough, then
f
exists on
[0,T_{0}]
, and
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}](T_{0})\leq C_{T_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}](0)
.

•

It allows to show that
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h-\mathbf{h}](T_{0})
is small. Using
T_{0}^{-1/2}
as a small parameter, we are then able to control
\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}-\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}](t)
for
t\geq T_{0}
. It allows us to prove that
f
is a global solution, and that
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h-\mathbf{h}](t)
remains small for all times.

•

Finally, our uniform bound on
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h]
allows to control
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f]
, for any
|\gamma|\leq N
, by elliptic estimates. Thus, in view of Remark
1.8
, we are able to show that the top order order derivatives of
f_{\circ}
grow logarithmically. From here, one can estimate
\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi_{f}]
, and then derive boundedness for
\mathbb{E}_{N}[h]
.

Since the above argument shows that both
\mathscr{W}_{+}
and
\mathscr{W}_{+}^{-1}
are locally Lipschitz with respect to the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[\cdot]
, it remains to prove that
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is a homeomorphism with respect to the stronger norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}[\cdot]
. To this end, we combine the previous ideas with standard arguments for proving local-in-time Cauchy stability for quasilinear PDEs (see, for instance,
[
1
, Section 4.5]
).

Remark 1.10
.

In the perspective of proving analogues of Theorems
1.3
and
1.6
for the Vlasov–Maxwell system, one expects to face the following difficulties.

•

The resonant phenomenon occurring between the electromagnetic field and particles with very high momentum. One way to deal with this issue is to lose powers of
(v,x-t\widehat{v})
, where
\widehat{v}\coloneqq v/\langle v\rangle
, in the estimates, which would be problematic in the present setting. We expect that this difficulty could be avoided by taking advantage of the null structure of the Lorentz force, described for instance in
[
4
, Lemma 4.1]
.

•

The lack of elliptic regularity. Because of this, the approach developed in the present paper cannot be adapted by setting
\Phi_{f}(t,v)\propto t^{2}[E+\widehat{v}\times B](t,x+t\widehat{v})
, since one would not be able to close the top order estimates. One expects to overcome this difficulty by exploiting the so-called Glassey–Strauss decomposition of the fields. However, the exact expression of
\Phi_{f}
remains to be determined.

•

Finding a suitable functional framework for the electromagnetic field. In contrast with (
VP
), the field satisfies an evolutionary equation and, in particular, exhibits linear scattering. One therefore has to identify a Banach space on which the scattering map acts as an endomorphism.

1.4  Outline of the paper

The remainder of the article is organised as follows.

•

Section
2
. We introduce the notation used throughout this paper and prove several decay estimates. In particular, we establish decay estimates for velocity averages of solutions to Vlasov equations, as well as for the associated force fields, adapted to our class of coordinate systems.

•

Section
3
. We study the properties of a transport operator in a dynamical or teleological coordinate system, where the force field is assumed to be sufficiently regular. We prove boundedness and convergence results which, in the analysis of solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system, correspond to modified scattering. We also show that the associated asymptotic Cauchy problem is well-posed. As an application, we study the long time dynamics of small data solutions to (
VP
) and prove Theorem
1.3
.

•

Section
4
. We show that the modified wave operator
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is a bijection between two open sets. More precisely, we prove that global solutions to (
VP
) that satisfy modified scattering are asymptotically stable under perturbations of both the initial data and the scattering data.

•

Section
5
. We conclude the proof of Theorem
1.6
by proving that
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is a homeomorphism between the spaces of scattering and initial data.

•

Appendix A
. We prove an energy estimate used to control top order derivatives for large data solutions. We also show higher-order estimates for spherically symmetric solutions to the repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system.

2
Preliminaries

2.1  Notations

Let, throughout this paper,
p>\frac{3}{2}
and
p_{\infty}\coloneqq\lfloor 3/p\rfloor+1
, so that the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
holds.

For a multi-index
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, we set
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}=\partial_{x^{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{x^{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{x^{3}}^{\alpha_{3}}
and
\partial_{v}^{\alpha}=\partial_{v^{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{v^{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{v^{3}}^{\alpha_{3}}
. We further denote the length of
\alpha
by
|\alpha|\coloneqq\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}
. For derivatives with respect to
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we use the convention that a multi-index
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{6}
is written as
\alpha=(\alpha_{x},\alpha_{v})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}\times\mathbb{N}^{3}
, and we set

\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}\coloneqq\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}},\qquad\qquad|\alpha|\coloneqq|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|.

The Hessian of a function
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
is denoted by
\nabla^{2}\phi
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
or
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we denote the Japanese bracket of
x
by
\langle x\rangle\coloneqq\sqrt{1+|x|^{2}}
.

When introducing a constant
C
, we will write
C[a_{1},\dots,a_{i}]>0
to indicate that it depends only on the parameters
a_{1},\dots,a_{i}
. Finally, we will use the notation
A\lesssim B
to mean that there exists a constant
C>0
such that
A\leq CB
. In practice, the constant
C
will only depend on
p
,
N
, the regularity order of the solutions,
n=(n_{x},n_{v})
, which measures the decay of the solutions at infinity, and
\mathbf{\Lambda}
, where
\mathbf{\Lambda}
quantifies the size of a reference solution. The dependence in
\mathbf{\Lambda}
can be removed if
\mathbf{\Lambda}
is small.

2.2  Elliptic estimates

We recall here classical results. We start by a weighted Sobolev inequality.

Proposition 2.1
.

Let
a\geq 0
. For any sufficiently regular function
\psi\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\langle t+|x|\rangle^{a}|\psi|(x)\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{p}}}\sum_{|\kappa|\leq p_{\infty}}\Big\|\langle t+|y|\rangle^{a+|\kappa|}\partial_{y}^{\kappa}\psi(y)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y})}.

Proof.

If
t+|x|\leq 1
, this follows from the standard Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Otherwise, we apply a local Sobolev inequality, in the ball
\{|y|\leq 1/2\}
, to the function

y\mapsto\big\langle t+|x+y(t+|x|)|\big\rangle^{a}\psi\big(x+y(t+|x|)\big).

Then, we observe that, on the domain of integration,
t+|x|\lesssim t+|x+y(t+|x|)|
, and we perform the change of variables
z(y)=x+y(t+|x|)
.

Next, we recall elliptic estimates.

Proposition 2.2
.

Assume that
\rho\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
is sufficiently regular and consider the unique solution to
\Delta\phi=\rho
such that
\phi(x)\to 0
as
|x|\to+\infty
. Then, the following estimates holds,

\displaystyle\big\|\langle x\rangle^{a}\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\langle x\rangle^{a}\rho(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})},\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{3}{p}<a<3\frac{p-1}{p},

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{3}\rho\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})},

where the last one holds uniformly in
\tau\geq 0
.

Proof.

For the first estimate, we use
1<p<\infty
and that
x\mapsto\langle x\rangle^{ap}
is in Muckenhoupt’s class
A_{p}
if
-3<ap<3(p-1)
. For the second one, we use that
\phi=|x|^{-1}\ast\rho
, and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}\langle\tau+|y|\rangle^{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},\qquad\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}\langle\tau+|y|\rangle^{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{2}(\tau^{-1}+1+|z-x/\tau|)^{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\tau^{2}},

where the last inequality holds for
|x|\leq\tau
.

We will in fact use consequences of this result. We recall that
p>3/2
.

Corollary 2.3
.

Let
\rho\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function and
\phi
be the unique solution to
\Delta\phi=\rho
. Then,
for any multi-index
\gamma
and all
\tau\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|\leq|\gamma|}\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{1+|\alpha|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})},

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{2+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(x)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|\leq|\gamma|+p_{\infty}}\big\|\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}+|\alpha|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

Proof.

We will use the relations
\Delta\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi=\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\rho
,

[\Delta,\Omega_{ij}]=0,\quad[\Delta,S]=2\Delta,\qquad\quad\;\Omega_{ij}\coloneqq x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}},\quad S\coloneqq\sum_{1\leq k\leq 3}x^{k}\partial_{x^{k}},\qquad\quad\;|x|^{2}\partial_{x^{k}}=x^{k}S+\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}x^{i}\Omega_{ik}.

The last identity in particular implies that, for any smooth function
\psi
,

\langle x\rangle^{|\gamma|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\psi\big|(x)\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}\big|Z^{\beta}\psi\big|(x)\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\gamma|}\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\psi\big|(x),

where the sum is taken over all differential operators
Z^{\beta}
obtained as compositions of at most
|\gamma|
vector fields in
\{\partial_{x^{1}},\partial_{x^{2}},\partial_{x^{3}},\Omega_{12},\Omega_{13},\Omega_{23},S\}
. As
[\partial_{x^{k}},S]=\partial_{x^{k}}
and
[\partial_{x^{k}},\Omega_{ij}]=\delta_{k,i}\partial_{x^{j}}-\delta_{k,j}\partial_{x^{i}}
, Proposition
2.2
, applied with
a=1
, yields

\big\|\langle x\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}\big\|\langle x\rangle\nabla^{2}_{x}Z^{\beta}\phi\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}\big\|\langle x\rangle Z^{\beta}\rho\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\gamma|}\big\|\langle x\rangle^{1+|\kappa|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\rho\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

We conclude the
L^{p}
estimate by using
\langle\tau+|x|\rangle\lesssim\tau+\langle x\rangle\lesssim\langle\tau+|x|\rangle
and Proposition
2.2
, applied with
a=0
. Using in addition Proposition
2.1
, we similarly obtain the
L^{\infty}
estimate.

2.3  Estimates for velocity averages

We fix, for the remainder of this section,
N\geq 2p_{\infty}-1
and a constant
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
. We consider further

•

A constant
a\geq 0
, and a function
(t,v)\mapsto\Phi(t,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, such that, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)\coloneqq\big\|\langle v\rangle^{2}\Phi(t,v)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\big\|\langle v\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle.

(H
\Phi
)

•

The functions, sometimes vue as variables,

z(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{Z}(t,y,v)\coloneqq y-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle.

In what follows, we will often write
z
for
z(t,x,v)
, and
\mathcal{Z}
for
\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)
.

•

A sufficiently regular function
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,z(t,x,v),v\big)
.

The estimates proved in this section will be applied to various choices of the function
\Phi
, including
\Phi_{f}
and
\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
, introduced in Section
1.3
. Although in most applications we will have
a=0
, the resulting logarithmic growth is harmless. Accordingly, we do not attempt to optimize the logarithmic losses in the estimates below. We start by relating the derivatives of
f
to the ones of
g
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\big[\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\lambda t\langle t\rangle^{-2}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}f\big]\big(t,z(t,x,v),v\big),

(9)

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}g(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\big[\partial_{x^{i}}f\big]\big(t,z(t,x,v),v\big),

\displaystyle\partial_{v^{i}}g(t,z,v)

\displaystyle=\big[t\partial_{x^{i}}f+\partial_{v^{i}}f+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}f\big]\big(t,z(t,x,v),v\big).

We will also make use of the function

f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+tv,v),\qquad\qquad\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)=\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\big(t\partial_{x}+\partial_{v}\big)^{\alpha_{v}}f\big](t,x+tv,v),

(10)

which corresponds to
f
composed by the linear flow of
\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}
, and coincides with
g
if
\Phi\equiv 0
. These relations allow to express a transport operator, defined in the coordinate system
(t,x,v)
, using the coordinates
(t,z,v)
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}
be a potential, and

\mathbf{T}_{\phi}\coloneqq\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}.

Then,
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)=F
if and only if
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=F(t,z(t,x,v),v)
, where

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}=\partial_{t}+\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,v)\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}+\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{v}.

Remark 2.5
.

For all choices that will be made in this paper,
\nabla_{v}\Phi
is a symmetric matrix-valued function. Consequently, there is no ambiguity in the notation
\nabla_{v}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}
, which stands for
\sum_{i,j}\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi^{j}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\partial_{x^{j}}
.

We now control the derivatives of
f_{\circ}
by the ones of
g
. Recall from Definition
1.1
the expression of
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. We have, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\circ}](t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)+\log^{(a+1)(2N+n_{x})-a}\hskip-0.59998pt\langle t+1\rangle\,\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Remark 2.7
.

Since
f_{\circ}
and
g
have symmetric roles, up to changing
\Phi
in
-\Phi
, we also have

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\lesssim\log^{(a+1)(2N+n_{x})}\hskip-0.59998pt\langle t+1\rangle\cdot\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\circ}](t).

Proof.

Let
|\alpha|\leq N
. Iterating (
9
), we can write
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g(t,x,v)-\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
as a linear combination of the terms

\log^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle t\rangle\,\overline{\Phi}(t,v)\,\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}g(t,x,v),\qquad\overline{\Phi}(t,v)=\prod_{1\leq i\leq|\beta_{x}|}\partial_{v^{j_{i}}}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{i}}\Phi^{k_{i}},\qquad\sum_{1\leq i\leq|\beta_{x}|}|\xi_{i}|+|\beta|=|\alpha_{v}|,\quad|\beta_{x}|\geq 1.

Then, if
|\beta_{x}|\geq 2
, we can bound all the factors in
\overline{\Phi}
but one since
\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
for any
|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
. This follows from (
H

Φ

), the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, and
N\geq 2p_{\infty}-1
. We obtain

\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g(t,x,v)-\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\big|\lesssim\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha_{v}|}\frac{\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi(t,v)\big|}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g\big|(t,x,v),

where
|\beta|\geq 1
in the sum, and
b\leq a(N-1)+N
. Next, we obtain, with
b^{\prime}\coloneqq(a+1)(2N+n_{x})-a
,

\bigg\|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}}\Big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)-\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g\big]\big(t,x-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim\log^{b^{\prime}}\!\langle t+1\rangle\,\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)

(11)

from the following properties.

(
a
)

We have
\big|\big\langle x-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle\big\rangle-\langle x\rangle\big|\leq\|\Phi(t,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\log\langle t\rangle\lesssim\log^{a+1}\langle t+1\rangle
.

(
b
)

If
|\xi|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, we bound
\langle v\rangle|\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{\xi}\Phi|(t,v)
by the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

(
c
)

If
|\alpha_{x}|+|\beta_{x}|+|\beta_{v}|\leq p_{\infty}-1
, we have, for almost all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\!\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{N+n_{v}}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|+|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g(t,x,v)\big|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\bigg\|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{N+n_{v}}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|+|\beta_{x}|}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g(t,x,v)\bigg\|_{W^{p_{\infty},p}_{v}L^{p}_{x}}^{p}\!\leq\big|\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\big|^{p},

by using the Sobolev embedding for Banach-valued functions
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
.

We conclude the proof using (
11
),
(a)
and by performing a change of variables.

We now relate the velocity averages of
f
to the ones of
g
.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
|\alpha|\leq N
. Then, denoting
\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)
by
\mathcal{Z}
, we have, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\langle t+|x|\rangle^{|\alpha|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho[f]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\bigg(1+\frac{\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi\big|(t,v)\bigg)\cdot\big\langle\mathcal{Z}\big\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g\big|\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v.

Proof.

Note that the case
t+|x|\leq 1
follows from
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f(t,x,v)=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}g(t,\mathcal{Z},v)
, so that we assume
t+|x|\geq 1
. To obtain the spatial decay, we found convenient to use, in this proof, a weighted-derivatives approach. We define by
\mathbb{G}
the set of the vector fields

G_{k}\coloneqq t\partial_{x^{k}},\qquad\Omega_{ij}\coloneqq x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}},\qquad S\coloneqq\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq 3}x^{\ell}\partial_{x^{\ell}},\qquad\qquad|x|^{2}\partial_{x^{k}}=x^{k}S+\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}x^{i}\Omega_{ik},

(12)

where
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
. We introduce further the set
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
of the
(x,v)
-weighted derivatives

\widehat{G}_{k}\coloneqq t\partial_{x^{k}}+\partial_{v^{k}},\qquad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\coloneqq x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}}+v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}},\qquad\widehat{S}\coloneqq\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq 3}x^{\ell}\partial_{x^{\ell}}+v^{\ell}\partial_{v^{\ell}}.

We consider an ordering on
\mathbb{G}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
. Moreover, given a multi-index
\gamma\in\llbracket 1,7\rrbracket^{q}
of length
|\gamma|=q
, we define
Z^{\gamma}
and
\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}
as
Z^{\gamma_{1}}\dots Z^{\gamma_{q}}
and
\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}=\widehat{Z}^{\gamma_{1}}\dots\widehat{Z}^{\gamma_{q}}
respectively. Then, using first (
12
), and performing then integration by parts, we get

(t+|x|)^{|\alpha|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho[f]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq|\alpha|}\big|Z^{\gamma}\rho[f]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\alpha|}\big|\rho\big[\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f\big]\big|(t,x).

(13)

It will be convenient to use that we can, without loss of generality, assume that
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}
is of the form

\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}=\widehat{G}^{\alpha_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\alpha_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{q},\qquad|\alpha_{v}|+|\alpha_{\Omega}|+q=|\kappa|,

where
G^{\alpha_{v}}=G_{1}^{\alpha_{v}^{1}}G_{2}^{\alpha_{v}^{2}}G_{3}^{\alpha_{v}^{3}}
, and
\Omega^{\alpha_{\Omega}}
is a differential operator of order
|\alpha_{\Omega}|
composed by the rotational vector fields
\Omega_{ij}
,
1\leq i<j\leq 3
. We can assume that since the only non trivial commutation relations between the elements of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
are

\displaystyle[\widehat{G}_{k},\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}]=\delta_{i,k}\widehat{G}_{j}-\delta_{j,k}\widehat{G}_{i},\quad\;\;[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij},\widehat{\Omega}_{k\ell}]=\delta_{j,k}\widehat{\Omega}_{i\ell}+\delta_{j,\ell}\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}+\delta_{i,\ell}\widehat{\Omega}_{jk}+\delta_{i,k}\widehat{\Omega}_{\ell j},\quad\;\;[\widehat{G}_{k},\widehat{S}]=\widehat{G}_{k},\quad\;\;[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij},\widehat{S}]=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}.

We fix such a differential operator
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}=\widehat{G}^{\alpha_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\alpha_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{q}
, with
|\kappa|\leq|\alpha|
, and let us write
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f
in terms of derivatives of
g
. We observe first

\widehat{G}_{k}f(t,x,v)=\partial_{v^{k}}f_{\circ}(t,x-tv,v),\qquad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f(t,x,v)=\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f_{\circ}\big](t,x-tv,v),\qquad\widehat{S}f(t,x,v)=\big[\widehat{S}f_{\circ}\big](t,x-tv,v),

so that

\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f(t,x,v)=\big[\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\alpha_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{q}f_{\circ}\big](t,x-tv,v).

(14)

Using that
f_{\circ}(t,x,v)=g(t,x-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
, we can write
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f(t,x,v)-\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\alpha_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{q}g(t,\mathcal{Z},v)
as a linear combination of the terms

\log^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle t\rangle\cdot\overline{\Phi}(t,v)\cdot\big[\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\beta_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{r}g\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v),v\big),\qquad\overline{\Phi}\coloneqq\prod_{1\leq i\leq|\beta_{x}|}\partial_{v}^{\gamma^{i}}\Omega^{\gamma_{\Omega}^{i}}S^{m^{i}}\Phi^{j_{i}},

where we have
1\leq j_{i}\leq 3
,
|\beta_{x}|\geq 1
, and

|\beta_{v}|+|\beta_{\Omega}|+r+\sum_{1\leq i\leq|\beta_{x}|}|\gamma^{i}|+|\gamma_{\Omega}^{i}|+m^{i}\leq|\alpha_{v}|+|\alpha_{\Omega}|+q,\qquad\qquad|\gamma^{i}|+|\gamma_{\Omega}^{i}|+m^{i}\geq 1.

The key idea is that a new factor of
\overline{\Phi}
is generated whenever
\widehat{G}_{k}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}
, or
\widehat{S}
differentiate the
\Phi(t,v)
-dependent part of
\mathcal{Z}
. This creates a new
x
-derivative applied to
g
. We now have to deal with the logarithmical growth of such terms. Let us show

\displaystyle\hskip-9.10486pt\bigg|\rho\big[\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f\big](t,x)-\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\!\big[\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\alpha_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{q}g\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|\!\lesssim\!\sum_{|\xi|+|\kappa^{\prime}|\leq|\kappa|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi\big|\cdot\langle\mathcal{Z}\rangle^{|\kappa^{\prime}_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa^{\prime}_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa^{\prime}}g\big|\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v,

(15)

which, in view of the inequality (
16
) stated below, will imply the result. For this, observe that, for a function
h
and since
\mathcal{Z}=x-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)=-\frac{1}{t}\Big(\partial_{v^{i}}\big[h(t,\mathcal{Z},v)\big]+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)-\partial_{v^{i}}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\Big).

We then exploit that
|\beta_{x}|\geq 1
, and we perform integration by parts. We then deal with the
\Phi
coefficients by noting that, for
1\leq|\gamma|+|\gamma_{\Omega}|+m\leq N+1
, we have

\big|\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Omega^{\gamma_{\Omega}}S^{m}\Phi^{j}\big|(t,v)\lesssim\sum_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|+|\gamma_{\Omega}|+m-1}\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{\xi}\Phi\big|(t,v).

Moreover, by (
H

Φ

), we have
\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi|\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
for all
|\xi|\leq N-p_{\infty}
. In particular, as
N\geq 2p_{\infty}-1
, we can estimate pointwise all but one
\Phi
factors. We finally obtain (
15
) by using
\log^{N+1+Na}\langle t+1\rangle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{1/4}
, and by noticing

\Big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\beta_{\Omega}}\widehat{S}^{r}g\Big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\kappa_{x}|+|\beta_{v}|+|\beta_{\Omega}|+r}\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g\big|(t,x,v).

(16)

For proving decay estimates, we will make use, throughout this paper, of the following change of variables.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
. Then, there exists
C[p]>0
such that, if
t>C\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a+1}\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle
, the map

v\mapsto x+tv+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle

is a
C^{1}
diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
onto
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, whose jacobian determinant is bounded between
t^{3}/2
and
2t^{3}
.

Proof.

This follows from (
H

Φ

), which implies that for
t
large enough,
v\mapsto v+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\frac{\log\langle t\rangle}{t}
is a
C^{1}
diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
onto
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, whose jacobian determinant is bounded between
1/2
and and
2
.

We prove a last result, which, together with Lemma
2.8
, will directly implies decay for
\rho[f]
.

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq N
. Then, for all
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big\langle\mathcal{Z}\big\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g\big|\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(6,6)}[g](t),

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi(t,v)\big|\cdot\big\langle\mathcal{Z}\big\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g\big|\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(6,6)}[g](t).

Proof.

Note first that if
|x|\geq 2\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a+1}\langle t+1\rangle
, we have
|x|\leq 2\langle t\rangle\langle\mathcal{Z}\rangle\langle v\rangle
since

|x|\leq\big|\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)\big|+t|v|+\|\Phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,v}}\log\langle t\rangle\leq\big|\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)\big|+t|v|+|x|/2.

We focus now on the velocity average of
h(t,\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v),v)
, for a sufficiently regular distribution function
h
. We have, using the previous estimate, together with the Hölder inequality,

\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|h|\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\leq\langle t\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)\rangle^{\frac{3p}{p-1}}\langle v\rangle^{\frac{3p}{p-1}}}\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\Big\|\langle\mathcal{Z}\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}\langle v\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\Big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}.

According to Lemma
2.9
, if
t
is large enough, we can perform the change of variables
y(v)=\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)
and obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}}\lesssim\frac{2}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{3}},\qquad\qquad\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle v\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}}<+\infty.

We then deduce that

\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|h|\big(t,\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v),v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\Big\|\langle x\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}\langle v\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}h(t,x,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

(17)

where we performed the change of variables
x^{\prime}(x)=\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)
. Then, we apply (
17
) to

h(t,x,v)=\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g,\qquad h(t,x,v)=\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial^{\xi}_{v}\Phi(t,v)\big|\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g,

for
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|
, and we recall from Definition
1.1
the expression of
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(6,6)}[\cdot]
. The latter quantities require an additional step to be bounded. If
|\xi|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, we use a Sobolev embedding to get
\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi]
. Otherwise
|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}-1
and by the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\langle x\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}\langle v\rangle^{6-\frac{3}{p}}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\langle x\rangle^{6-\frac{1}{p}+|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{6-\frac{1}{p}+|\beta_{v}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g\big\|_{W^{p_{\infty},p}_{v}L^{p}_{x}}\cdot\big\|\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}}.

Combining Lemmata
2.8
and
2.10
with (
H

Φ

) yields the following result.

Proposition 2.11
.

For any
|\alpha|\leq N
and all
t\geq 0
, we have

\Big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}+|\alpha|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho[f](t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(6,6)}[g](t).

2.4  Estimates for the force field

We start by studying the asymptotic force field, defined as the solution to
\Delta\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]=\int_{x}f_{\infty}(x,\cdot)\mathrm{d}x
, for a sufficiently regular function
f_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 2.12
.

We have, for any
|\alpha|\leq N
,

\big\|\langle v\rangle^{1+|\alpha|}\nabla^{2}_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\alpha|}\big\|\langle x\rangle^{3}\langle v\rangle^{1+|\kappa|}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

For any
|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, we have

\langle v\rangle^{2+|\gamma|}\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]\big|(v)\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\gamma|+p_{\infty}}\big\|\langle x\rangle^{3}\langle v\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Proof.

This follows from the elliptic estimates of Corollary
2.3
, applied with
\tau=0
, and the Hölder inequality.

We now control the force field
\nabla_{x}\phi[f]
, satisfying
\Delta\phi[f]=\rho[f]
. We note
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi[f]=\phi[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f]
.

Proposition 2.13
.

Let
|\alpha|\leq N
. For all
t\geq 0
, we have

\langle t\rangle^{\frac{2p-3}{p}}\Big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{1+|\alpha|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big]\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t).

For any
|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, we have, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
,

\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2+|\gamma|}\big|\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t).

Proof.

For both inequalities, we apply the elliptic estimates of Corollary
2.3
together with Proposition
2.11
.

2.4.1 Improved estimates for the force field

Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular potential and recall from Lemma
2.4
the definition of
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}
. We assume now that
f
is a solution to
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)=0
and let us show that improved estimates for
\phi[f]
can be obtained. Compared with Proposition
2.13
, we will control one derivative less.

Lemma 2.14
.

Recall that
f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+tv,v)
. Then, almost everywhere and for any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\big[\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y=0.

Proof.

We use that
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)=0
, so that
\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}f
, and

\partial_{t}f_{\circ}=\big[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f\big](t,x+tv,v),\qquad\partial_{x^{i}}f_{\circ}=\partial_{x^{i}}f(t,x+tv,v),\qquad\partial_{v^{i}}f_{\circ}=\big[t\partial_{x^{i}}f+\partial_{v^{i}}f\big](t,x+tv,v).

By Leibniz formula, we then deduce

\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}=\lambda\sum_{\gamma+\beta=\alpha}{\alpha\choose\beta}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}-{\alpha\choose\beta}t^{|\gamma|+1}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}.

It remains to evaluate this relation at
(t,y,v-y/t)
and to note that

t\nabla_{y}\Big[\big[\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\Big]=t\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)-\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big).

In the next two results, we will make use of the relation

t^{3}\rho[h](t,x)=t^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}h(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}h_{\circ}\Big(t,y,\frac{x-y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y,

(18)

which is obtained by performing the change of variables
y=x-tv
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N-1
. Then, for all
t\geq 1
, we have

\Big\|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\Big[t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\Big]\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t\rangle}{t^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),

where
b\coloneqq(a+1)(2N+7)
. For any
|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}
and all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle v\rangle\Big|\partial_{t}\Big[t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f\big](t,tv)\Big]\Big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t\rangle}{t^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t).

Proof.

The proof relies on elliptic estimates, the relation
\partial_{v^{i}}[\psi(tv)]=t\partial_{x^{i}}\psi(tv)
, and

\Delta_{v}\big[\partial_{t}\big(t^{1+|\gamma|}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big)\big]=\partial_{t}\big(t^{3+|\gamma|}\rho\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big)=\partial_{t}\big(t^{3}\rho\big[(t\partial_{x}+\partial_{v})^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big).

Recall that
\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)=[(t\partial_{x}+\partial_{v})^{\gamma}f](t,x+tv,v)
, so that (
18
), applied to
h=(t\partial_{x}+\partial_{v})^{\gamma}f
, yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big(t^{3}\rho\big[(t\partial_{x}+\partial_{v})^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\big[\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)+\frac{y}{t^{2}}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y.

According to the previous Lemma
2.14
, the first term on the right hand side vanishes. For the second term, we apply Hölder inequality in
y
to get

\bigg\|\langle v\rangle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{y}{t^{2}}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{v}}\leq\frac{1}{t^{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle\langle y\rangle^{4}\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{p}_{y,v}}=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle\langle y\rangle^{5}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\circ}(t,y,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}_{y,v}},

where, in the last step, we performed a change of variables and use the inequality
\langle z+w\rangle\leq\langle z\rangle\langle w\rangle
.
The last term can then be expressed in terms of
g
through Lemma
2.6
. It allows to derive the
L^{p}
bound by the elliptic estimates of Proposition
2.2
, applied with
a=1
.

By the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, we get the pointwise bounds for
|\kappa|\geq 1
. For the last case, we will use the pointwise bound in Proposition
2.2
for
\tau=0
. For this, observe that by using first a Sobolev embedding in
v
, and then the Hölder inequality in
y
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{y}{t^{2}}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}f_{\circ}\big]

\displaystyle\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\leq\sum_{|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}y\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{v}}

(19)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\langle y\rangle^{4}\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big]\Big(t,y,v-\frac{y}{t}\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{y,v}}\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\langle y\rangle^{7}\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\circ}\big](t,y,v)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{y,v}}.

We conclude by using again Lemma
2.6
.

We complement here the last result by dealing with the small times. It will in particular allow to control the error terms of (
type–

3

) in the commutation formula determined below. We use again the constant
b\coloneqq(a+1)(2N+7)
.

Lemma 2.16
.

Assume
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
. We have, for all
(t,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\Big[t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\Big]\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle p_{\infty}-1\leq|\gamma|\leq N-1,

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle\Big|\partial_{t}\Big[t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f\big](t,tv)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}.

Proof.

In view of the previous Lemma
2.15
, it suffices to deal with small times
0\leq t\leq 1
. We will use the relation

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle\big|\partial_{t}\big(t^{2+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t\langle v\rangle\big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)\big|+t^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2}\big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f](t,tv)\big|

\displaystyle\quad\,+t\langle v\rangle\big|t^{1+|\gamma|}\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f](t,tv)\big|.

(20)

We then need to control
\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}\phi[f]
. We exploit for this the conservation of the number of particles. More precisely, as
\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\nabla_{v}\cdot\big(f\nabla_{x}\phi\big)=0
, we have

\Delta\partial_{t}\phi[f]=\partial_{t}\rho[f]=-\nabla_{x}\cdot\rho[vf]=-\rho\big[v\cdot\nabla_{x}f\big].

Note further that
v\cdot\nabla_{x}f(t,z(t,x,v),v)=v\cdot\nabla_{x}g(t,x,v)
. Then, we can apply Proposition
2.11
to estimate
\rho\big[vf\big]
and its derivatives up to order
N
. Then, proceeding as in Proposition
2.13
, one obtains through elliptic estimates that, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
,

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle^{\frac{2p-3}{p}}\Big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2+|\alpha|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{t}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big]\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),\qquad

\displaystyle|\alpha|\leq N-1,

(21)

\displaystyle\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{t}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f\big]\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\kappa|\leq N-1-p_{\infty},

(22)

where we used that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]=\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(7,7)}[g]
.

Using
t\langle x/t\rangle\leq t+|x|
, Proposition
2.13
, and (
22
), we obtain the pointwise decay estimates for
|\gamma|\leq N-1-p_{\infty}
. For the
L^{p}
estimate, note that for a function
\psi(x)
,

\big\|t^{s}\langle v\rangle^{s}t^{q}\psi(tv)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq t^{q-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{s}\psi(x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})},\qquad q,\,s\geq 0.

This is obtained from the change of variables
x=tv
, and
t\langle x/t\rangle=(t^{2}+|x|^{2})^{1/2}
. Using Proposition
2.13
and (
21
), we can then bound in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, uniformly in
0\leq t\leq 1
, the three terms in the right hand side of (
20
), provided that
|\gamma|\geq 3/p
. We finally derive the pointwise estimates for
|\kappa|=N-p_{\infty}
through a Sobolev embedding.

We can now prove that, for suitable functions
f
,
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
converges as
t\to+\infty
. Recall for this that
\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
is the unique potential, going to
0
as
|x|\to+\infty
, satisfying
\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]=\int_{x}f_{\infty}(x,\cdot)\mathrm{d}x
.

Corollary 2.17
.

Assume that
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
, that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]
is uniformly bounded, and that there exists
f_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\big\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\langle x\rangle^{4}\big(g(t,x,v)-f_{\infty}(x,v)\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}=0.

Then, for all
t\geq 0
and any
p_{\infty}-1\leq|\gamma|\leq N-1
,

\Big\|\langle v\rangle\Big(t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,tv)-\nabla^{2}_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](v)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](\tau),

where
b\coloneqq(a+1)(2N+7)
. Moreover, for all
(t,v)\in[1,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and any
|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}
,

\langle v\rangle\Big|t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f\big](t,tv)-\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](\tau).

Proof.

The assumption on
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]
, together with the previous Lemma
2.15
and the Minkowski integral inequality, imply that
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
converges with the stated rate of convergence.

To identify the limit, we note that (
18
) and the fundamental theorem of calculus imply

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}t^{3}\rho[f](t,tv)-\langle v\rangle^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}y\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{\circ}\Big(t,y,v-\theta\frac{y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t),

where, in the last step, we proceed as in (
19
). Using that the spatial average of
g
and
f_{\circ}
correspond and

\Big\|\langle v\rangle^{2}\nabla_{v}\Big(t\phi[f](t,tv)-\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\bigg\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg(t^{3}\rho[f](t,tv)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}f_{\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg)\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

which follows from Proposition
2.2
, we finally derive that
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
converges uniformly to
\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
.

2.5  Commutation formula

Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a potential. The goal is to compute the commutators of
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
, the transport operator introduced in Lemma
2.4
. To simplify the notation, we introduce the following convention.

Definition 2.18
.

For
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\leq N
, we will denote by
P_{r,q}(\Phi)
a quantity of the form

\prod_{1\leq k\leq r}\partial_{v^{j_{k}}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{k}}\Phi^{i_{k}}(t,v),\qquad\qquad\sum_{1\leq k\leq r}|\alpha_{k}|=q,\qquad 1\leq i_{k},\,j_{k}\leq 3,

where
P_{0,0}(\Phi)=1
by convention.

We obtain the first order commutation formula by using the expression of
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
given in Lemma
2.4
, that
\partial_{x^{i}}z^{j}=\delta_{i,j}
, and
\partial_{v^{i}}z^{j}=t\delta_{i,j}+\lambda\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi^{j}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
.

Lemma 2.19
.

We have

\displaystyle\big[\mathbf{T}_{\phi},\partial_{x^{i}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\lambda t\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}-\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}+\lambda\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{v}

and

\displaystyle\big[\mathbf{T}_{\phi},\partial_{v^{i}}\big]=

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}t\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)-\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}-t\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\big(\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\big)\cdot\nabla_{x}

\displaystyle-\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}-t\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{x}

\displaystyle-\sum_{1\leq k\leq 3}\lambda\log^{2}\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}\Phi^{k}\cdot\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi(t,z)\cdot\partial_{x^{k}}+\lambda t\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{v}+\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi(t,z)\cdot\nabla_{v}.

By iterating the above, one can derive the following high order commutation relation.

Corollary 2.20
.

Let
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N
. Then,
\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi},\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\big]
is a linear combination of the following terms.

•

The ones of (
type–

1

)

\displaystyle\frac{t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}},\qquad\qquad|\gamma|\geq 1,\quad\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v}.

(
type
–
1
)

•

The ones of (
type–

2

), with the convention that
\partial_{x^{k}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{k}}^{1-\delta}=\partial_{x^{k}}
if
\delta=1
and
\partial_{x^{k}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{k}}^{1-\delta}=\partial_{v^{k}}
if
\delta=0
,

\displaystyle t^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}\log^{r}\langle t\rangle P_{r,q}(\Phi)\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\cdot\partial_{x^{k}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{k}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}},

(
type
–
2
)

where
r\leq|\alpha_{v}|+1
and
q+|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|
. Moreover, we have
|\beta_{x}|\leq|\alpha_{x}|
,
|\beta_{v}|\leq|\alpha_{v}|
,
|\beta|<|\alpha|
, and the additional conditions

1.

\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q\geq 0
, so that there are no negative powers of
t
,

2.

if
|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|
, then
r\geq 1
.

•

The ones of (
type–

3

), which vanish if
\Phi
does not depend on
t
,

\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}(\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}},\qquad\qquad|\gamma|\geq 1,\quad\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v}

(
type
–
3
)

Proof.

We argue by induction on
|\alpha|=|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|
and we note that the first order case
|\alpha|=1
is treated by Lemma
2.19
. In particular, for the second additional condition for the terms of (
type–

2

), the case
|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|
can only occur when commuting with
\partial_{v^{i}}
. Assume now that the result holds for all multi-indices of length
m
, and let
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|=m+1
. Write

\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}=\partial_{w}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}},\qquad|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|=m,

where
\partial_{w}
denotes either one space derivative
\partial_{x^{i}}
or one velocity derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
. Then

\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]},\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\big]=\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]},\partial_{w}\big]\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}+\partial_{w}\Big(\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]},\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\big]\Big).

The first term is of the required form by the first order commutation formula of Lemma
2.19
. It then remains to check that the three classes of terms are stable under one additional derivative.

If
\partial_{w}=\partial_{x^{i}}
, then no new factor of
t
,
\log\langle t\rangle
, or
\Phi
is created, since
\partial_{x^{j}}z^{k}=\delta_{j,k}
. As a consequence, differentiating any term of (
type–

1

), (
type–

2

), or (
type–

3

) simply increases the number of
x
-derivatives falling either on
\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi
or on the final differential operator. The structure is thus preserved.

Assume now that
\partial_{w}=\partial_{v^{i}}
. Then, there are four cases to consider.

•

The derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
hits the differential operator
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}
or
\partial_{x^{k}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{k}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}
. Then
|\beta_{v}|
increases by one, which preserves the structure of the terms (
type–

1

)–(
type–

3

).

•

The derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
hits the factor
P_{r,q}(\Phi)
. In that case, this produces
r
quantities of the form
P_{r,q+1}(\Phi)
. Thus the structure of the terms of (
type–

2

) is again preserved.

•

The derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
hits the force field
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)
. Using
\partial_{v^{i}}z^{j}=t\delta_{ij}+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\,\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi^{j}
,
we obtain

\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\big]=t\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi^{j}\partial_{x^{j}}\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z).

Hence, when the velocity derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
falls on
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)
, it can only

(i)

produce one additional power of
t
, together with one more
x
-derivative on
\phi
,

(ii)

or produce one additional factor
\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi
, and again one more
x
-derivative on
\phi
.

For the terms of (
type–

2

), this is exactly the origin of the exponent for the factor
t^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}
, and this proves that the constraint
q+|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|
is preserved.

A term of (
type–

1

) gives rise to quantity with the same structure when
(i)
occurs. Indeed, in that case
\partial_{v^{i}}
also hits
\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi
. If
(ii)
occurs, it gives rise to a (
type–

2

) term.

•

The derivative
\partial_{v^{i}}
hits
\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi
. In that case, the structure of the terms of (
type–

3

) is preserved.

Therefore each additional derivative sends any admissible term of order
m
into a linear combination of terms of (
type–

1

)–(
type–

3

) of order
m+1
. This concludes the induction.

In practice, and in view of the hirarchised norms that we will propagate, we will appeal to the next result.

Corollary 2.21
.

We can bound
\langle x\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\alpha_{x}|}\big|\big[\mathbf{T}_{\phi},\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\big](g)\big|
by a linear combination of the terms

\displaystyle\frac{\langle v\rangle}{\langle t\rangle\langle x\rangle}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}g\big|,\qquad

\displaystyle|\gamma|\geq 1,\quad\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v},\quad|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|+1,

\displaystyle\log^{Q}\!\langle t+1\rangle\big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi|\big)\Big|\langle t+|z|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\Big|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}g\big|,\quad\;

\displaystyle|\gamma|+|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|+1,\;\;|\xi|,\,|\gamma|,\,|\beta|\leq|\alpha|,

\displaystyle\log\langle t\rangle\big|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\big(\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi\big)\big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}g\big|,\qquad

\displaystyle|\gamma|\geq 1,\quad\big(|\gamma|-1\big)+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|,

where
Q=(a+1)N+1
and
|\gamma|\leq|\alpha|
.

Proof.

We will use the following inequality, which follows from
z=x+tv+\Phi\log\langle t\rangle
and
|\Phi|\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
,

\displaystyle\langle x\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle z\rangle+t\,\langle v\rangle+\log^{a+1}\langle t+1\rangle\lesssim\langle t+|z|\rangle\,\langle v\rangle.

(23)

We consider first the terms of (
type–

2

). Since
\|\nabla_{v}\Phi\|_{W^{N-p_{\infty},\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
by (
H

Φ

), we have for
1\leq r\leq N+1
,

\big|P_{r,q}(\Phi)\big|\lesssim\log^{(r-1)a}\langle t+1\rangle\big|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi\big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{Na}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle v\rangle}\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi\big|,\qquad\qquad|\xi|\leq q.

(24)

We then need to bound

\mathcal{T}\coloneqq\langle t\rangle^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}\big|\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\big|\cdot\langle x\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\alpha_{x}|}\big|\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}g\big|,\qquad\quad\delta\in\{0,1\},\quad|\beta_{x}|\leq|\alpha_{x}|,\quad|\beta_{v}|\leq|\alpha_{v}|,

q+|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|
, and
|\beta|<|\alpha|
. We note that we used the first additional condition, namely that no term of (
type–

2

) involves negative powers of
t
. Let
\kappa
such that
\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}=\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}
.

•

Assume first that
\delta=1
and
|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|
, so that
|\kappa_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|+1
, and
r\geq 1
by the second additional condition. We have

\mathcal{T}=\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{1+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}\big|\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\big|\cdot\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|-1}\langle v\rangle^{-|\kappa_{x}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g\big|.

Then, note that in that case,
|\gamma|=|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-q
, and
1-r\leq 0
. Moreover, the extra factor
\langle v\rangle
is absorbed by (
24
) since
r\geq 1
.

•

Otherwise
|\beta_{x}|+\delta=|\kappa_{x}|\leq|\alpha_{x}|
and we use (
23
) to get

\mathcal{T}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}\langle t+|z|\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|-|\kappa_{x}|}\big|\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\big|\cdot\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\kappa_{x}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g\big|.

As
|\gamma|=|\alpha|-|\beta|-q
, we have
\langle t\rangle^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-r-q}\langle t+|z|\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|-|\kappa_{x}|}\leq\langle t+|z|\rangle^{|\gamma|-r}
, which allows to conclude the analysis of this error term.

For the terms of (
type–

1

) and (
type–

3

), we simply observe that the differential operator applied to
g
is
\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}=\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}
, where
|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|+1
. In the latter case, we additionnaly use
\langle x\rangle^{-1}\leq 1
.

2.6  Energy inequality

The next energy inequality will be used for both the forward and backward problems.

Proposition 2.22
.

Let
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq+\infty
. Then, for any
p\geq 1
,
