\Big|\big\|g(t_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}-\big\|g(t_{2},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\Big|\leq p\int_{t=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}t.

Proof.

We start by working with the function
f
. As
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}
, integration by parts provide

\displaystyle\Big|\big\|f(t_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}-\big\|f(t_{2},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}\Big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{t=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{T}_{\phi}\big(|f|^{p}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq p\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\big\|f(t_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p-1}\int_{t=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big\|\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}t,

where, in the last step, we used
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(|f|^{p})=p\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)f|f|^{p-2}
and the Hölder inequality. It allows to derive the result, since the
L^{p}
norms of
f
(respectively
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)
) and
g
(respectively
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)
) coincide, by the change of variables
x\mapsto x+tv+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
.

3
Analysis of the transport operator
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}

The purpose of this section is to study, given a sufficiently regular potential
\phi
and function
\Phi
, the properties of the solutions to
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=0
arising from sufficiently regular initial or scattering data. In particular, we will show boundedness for
g
, convergence as
t\to+\infty
, and that the asymptotic Cauchy problem is well-posed.

3.1  Assumptions on the force field

Let, for all this section,
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
. We begin by specifying the class of potentials
\phi
and coordinates
(t,z(t,x,v),v)
under consideration. Our motivation for considering such a class of potential is given in Remark
3.6
below.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
I
be an interval and
t\mapsto\Lambda_{t}
be a nonnegative function. We will say that the couple
(\phi,\Phi)
, composed by a potential
\phi\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
and a function
\Phi\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
, belongs to the classe
\mathcal{W}^{N}(I,\Lambda)
if it satisfies the following decay estimates.

•

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}
,
|\gamma|\leq N
, and all
(t,x)\in I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
,

\displaystyle\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2+|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{t},

(
\phi
,
L^{\infty}
, weak)

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{t}.

(
\phi
,
L^{p}
, weak)

•

For all
t\in I
,

\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)=\Big\|\langle v\rangle^{2}\Phi(t,v)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\Big\|\langle v\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\Lambda_{t}.

(
\Phi
, bound)

•

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}
,
p_{\infty}\leq|\alpha|\leq N
, and all
(t,v)\in I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,tv)-\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi(t,v)\Big|+\langle v\rangle\Big|\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi\Big|(t,v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}},

(
L^{\infty}\hskip-0.85358pt
, sharp)

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\frac{t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{1+|\alpha|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi(t,tv)-\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\Phi(t,v)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\!+\Big\|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\Phi(t,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}.

(
L^{p}\hskip-0.85358pt
, sharp)

Associated to the function
\Phi
, we define the coordinate system
(t,z(t,x,v),v)
, where

z(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle.

We will often write
z
instead of
z(t,x,v)
.

Remark 3.2
.

By (

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) and the weighted Sobolev embedding of Proposition
2.1
, we have

\langle t\rangle^{2}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi\big|(t,x)\lesssim\Lambda_{t},\qquad\qquad|\kappa|=N+1-p_{\infty}.

Let us show two results, which motivate Definition
3.1
. The next one will be useful in Sections
3.4
and
4.3
when we will perform a bootstrap argument during the analysis of a solution
f
to the Vlasov-Poisson system.

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
f\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
\Phi\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
be two functions, and assume that for all
t\in I
,

\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle,\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)<+\infty,

for some constants
a,\,\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
, where the function
g
is defined by

g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

Let further
\Phi_{f}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
. Then, there exists
C[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},a]>0
such that
(\phi[f],\Phi_{f})\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(I,C\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]\big)
.

Remark 3.4
.

In practice, we will apply Corollary
3.3
either with
\Phi=0
, or with
\Phi=\Phi_{f}
.

Proof.

The assumptions allow us to work within the framework of Section
2
.

•

We obtain (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) from Proposition
2.13
.

•

Then, by the change of variables
x=tv
and
t\langle x/t\rangle\leq t+|x|
, there holds

\Big\|\langle v\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}t^{3+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f](t,tv)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq t^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\Big\|(t+|x|)^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f](t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

(25)

Note then that
t^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\leq\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}
since
p>3/2
. Also, we have
\langle v\rangle^{2}|\Phi_{f}(t,v)|\leq(t+|tv|)^{2}|\nabla_{x}\phi[f]|(t,tv)
by using
t\langle v\rangle\leq t+t|v|
. As (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) holds, this gives (

Φ

, bound
).

•

The estimates for
\partial_{t}\Phi
are given by Lemma
2.16
. Finally, the last estimates to establish consists in controlling

\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi[f](t,tv)-\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi(t,v)\Big|=\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|t^{|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi[f](t,tv)\Big|.

For the
L^{\infty}
case, we use again
t\langle v\rangle\leq t+t|v|
and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
). For the
L^{p}
estimate, we perform the change of variables
x=tv
to get , for
p_{\infty}-1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle t^{2+|\alpha|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi[f](t,tv)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big\|(t+|x|)t^{1+|\alpha|-\frac{3}{p}}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi[f](t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t^{1+|\alpha|-\frac{3}{p}}}{\langle t\rangle^{4+|\alpha|-\frac{3}{p}}}\cdot\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\Big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{1+|\alpha|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi[f](t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

(26)

Then, to bound the right hand side for
0\leq t\leq 1
, we require
|\alpha|\geq p_{\infty}-1=\lfloor 3/p\rfloor
, and we use (

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
).

We now prove a similar result, which will be useful for the construction of solutions arising from scattering data. In that setting, the coordinate system used to control the solutions is teleological rather than dynamical.

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
f_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and
f\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be two functions, and assume that

\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]=\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall\,t\in I,\quad\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)<+\infty,

for some constant
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
, where the function
g
is defined by

g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

Then, there exists
C[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that
\big(\phi[f],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(I,C(\mathbf{\Lambda}+\mathbb{E}_{N}[g])\big)
.

Proof.

The strategy is slightly different than the one of the previous proof.

•

According to Proposition
2.12
, there exists
C_{1}[N,p]>0
such that
\mathcal{E}_{N}\big[\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]\big]\leq C_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}
. In particular, (

Φ

, bound
) holds, which allows us to apply the results of Section
2
with
\Phi=\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
.

•

We obtain that
\phi[f]
satisfies (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) from Proposition
2.13
.

•

For (

L
∞

, sharp
)–(

L
p

, sharp
), note first that
\partial_{t}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]=0
. Then, write

\displaystyle\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi[f](t,tv)-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\langle v\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\Big|t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi[f](t,tv)-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|t^{|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi[f](t,tv)\big|.

The first term on the right hand side can be estimated using Corollary
2.17
. For the second term, we apply the final step in the proof of the previous Corollary
3.3
. See in particular (
26
).

Remark 3.6
.

In this paper, in addition to the cases previously discussed, we will consider two other pairs
(\phi,\Phi)
.

•

A potential of the form
\phi[f]
, with
f
a solution to (
VP
), and
\Phi\equiv 0
. In this case,
\Lambda_{t}
will be proportional to
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}]
, and allowed to grow logarithmically.

•

A potential of the form
\phi[f-\mathbf{f}]
, with
f
and
\mathbf{f}
two solutions to (
VP
), and a function
\Phi=\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
. In this case,
\Lambda_{t}
will be proportional to
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)
.

We fix, for all this section, an interval
I\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}
, a nonnegative function
t\mapsto\Lambda_{t}
, and
(\phi,\Phi)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(I,\Lambda\big)
. We assume further that there exists
\mathbf{\Lambda},\,a\geq 0
such that
\Lambda_{t}\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
, so that (

Φ

, bound
) allows us to work within the framework of Section
2
.

3.2  Energy estimates

Motivated by the definition of the norms
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
, we introduce the following shorthand notation.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
|\alpha|\leq N
,
(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and
\mathfrak{g}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
. We define the function
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
as

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\mathfrak{g}(t,x,v).

We now prove a result which will in particular allow to prove boundedness for the solutions to
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=0
.

Proposition 3.8
.

Recall that
(\phi,\Phi)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(I,\Lambda)
. Let
g\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function, and
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Then, there exists a constant
C_{0}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that,

\forall\,t\in I,\qquad\quad\Big\|\Big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi},\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\Big](g)(t,\cdot,\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq C_{0}\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Remark 3.9
.

As can be checked from the proof of Corollary
2.21
, the dependence of
C_{0}
on
\mathbf{\Lambda}
is polynomial. More precisely, there exists
C_{1}[N,n,p]>0
and
q[N]\in\mathbb{N}
such that
C_{0}=C_{1}\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle^{q}
.

Proof.

Note first that, for all
t\in I
and all
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\big|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(\langle v\rangle\big)\big|\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}},\qquad\qquad\big|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(\langle x\rangle\big)\big|\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\langle x\rangle.

(27)

Indeed, using the expression for
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
provided by Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(\langle v\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\frac{v}{\langle v\rangle},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(\langle x\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,v)\Big)\cdot\frac{x}{\langle x\rangle}-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\Phi\cdot\frac{x}{\langle x\rangle}+\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi(t,v)\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)\cdot\frac{x}{\langle x\rangle}.

It remains to use (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
) and (

L
∞

, sharp
), which in particular imply, together with the mean value theorem,

\frac{t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,z)-\Phi(t,v)\Big|\lesssim t\Big|\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,z)-\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)\Big|+\Lambda_{t}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\langle x\rangle.

(28)

As a consequence, we have

\Big\|\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi},g_{\alpha}\big](t,\cdot,\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\big\|g_{\alpha}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\Big\|\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi},\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g\big](t,\cdot,\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}.

According to Corollary
2.21
, we are then lead to bound the quantities

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{t\langle v\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}\langle x\rangle}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\Big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\langle v\rangle\big|\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\big|\cdot g_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

where
1\leq|\gamma|\leq|\alpha|
and
\big(|\gamma|-1\big)+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|
, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\Big(1+\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi(t,v)\big|\Big)\Big|\langle t+|z|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\Big|\cdot g_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

where
|\xi|,\,|\gamma|,\,|\beta|\leq|\alpha|
and
|\xi|+|\gamma|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|+1
. We start by dealing with
\mathfrak{I}_{2}
.

(a)

Assume first that
|\xi|,\,|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
. Then, by (

Φ

, bound
), together with a Sobolev embedding, and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
),

\mathfrak{I}_{2}\lesssim(1+\Lambda_{t})\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|g_{\beta}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq(1+\Lambda_{t})\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

(b)

Assume now that
|\xi|\geq N+1-p_{\infty}
. Then,
|\gamma|+|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}
so we can still estimate the force field pointwise. We use in addition the Sobolev embedding for Banach-valued functions

to get

, so that

\mathfrak{I}_{2}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|g_{\beta}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi(t,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{v}}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|g_{\beta}\big|^{p}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg\|^{\frac{1}{p}}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim(1+\Lambda_{t})\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

(c)

Otherwise, we have
|\gamma|\geq N+1-p_{\infty}
so that
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}
and
\langle v\rangle|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi|(t,v)\lesssim\Lambda_{t}
. We start by performing the affine change of variables
y(x)=z(t,x,v)
. It yields

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\Lambda_{t})\Big\|\langle t+|y|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,y)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y})}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|g_{\beta}\big|^{p}\big(t,y-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y})}^{\frac{1}{p}}.

We estimate the force field using (

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
). For the distribution function, according to Lemma
2.9
, we can perform the change of variables
x(v)=y-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
for
t\geq t_{0}[p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]
, where
t_{0}\geq 1
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(1+\Lambda_{t})\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}}\cdot\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3}{p}}}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|g_{\beta}\big|^{p}(t,x,w)\mathrm{d}x\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{w})}^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\frac{(1+\Lambda_{t})\Lambda_{t}}{t^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Otherwise, we have
0\leq t\leq t_{0}
. Then, by a Sobolev embedding, and using that
t

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\Lambda_{t})\Lambda_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-2+\frac{3}{p}}\big\|g_{\beta}\big\|_{W^{p_{\infty},p}_{x}L^{p}_{v}}\lesssim(1+\Lambda_{t})\Lambda_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-2}\,\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

We conclude the analysis of
\mathfrak{I}_{2}
using
1+\Lambda_{t}\leq 1+\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
. We now focus on
\mathfrak{I}_{1}
, and we note that
\mathfrak{I}_{1}\leq\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{1}+\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle}\Big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)-t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,tv)\Big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

(
\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{1}
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,tv)-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\Big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

If
|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, we estimate pointwise the force field using (

L
∞

, sharp
) to get

\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\big\|g_{\beta}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Otherwise
|\gamma|\geq N+1-p_{\infty}
, so that

and

Next, we deal with
\mathfrak{I}_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle}\big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)-t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,tv)\big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{D}_{t})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle}\big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,tv)\big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle}\big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,z)\big|\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})}.

•

The domain
\{t_{1}+|v|\leq\sqrt{t}\}
.
The fundamental theorem of calculus yields

\mathfrak{Q}_{1}=\bigg\|\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\frac{t\langle v\rangle|x+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle|}{\langle x\rangle}\Big|t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\Big(t,tv+\theta\big(x+\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle\big)\Big)\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta\cdot g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{D}_{t})}.

Then, we note that on

∞

, sharp

We now consider the case

, so that

. The analysis shares some similarities with the one carried out in

. If

•

The integral
\mathfrak{Q}_{2}
, over the domain

Assume first that

, so that (

ϕ

,

L

, weak
) and Remark

give

\mathfrak{Q}_{2}\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle t+t|v|\rangle^{2}}g_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})}\leq\Lambda_{t}\big\|\langle t+t|v|\rangle^{-1}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})}\big\|g_{\beta}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t),

where we use

and that

on

so that we can use again

Note now that
|\gamma|\geq 3
. Therefore, performing the change of variables

and using then (

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
), we obtain

\mathfrak{Q}_{2}\lesssim\frac{t^{2}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}-1}t^{\frac{3}{p}}}\Big\|(t+|x|)^{|\gamma|-1}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\frac{t^{2-\frac{3}{p}}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{|\gamma|}{2}-1}\langle t\rangle^{3-\frac{3}{p}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

•

The integral
\mathfrak{Q}_{3}
.
Assume first
|\gamma|\leq N+1-p_{\infty}
, so that by (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
) and Remark
3.2
,

\mathfrak{Q}_{3}\lesssim\Lambda_{t}\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})}\big\|g_{\beta}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t),

provided that, for all
t\in I
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
and
|v|\geq\sqrt{t}-t_{1}
,

\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

(29)

As
t\langle v\rangle\leq t+t|v|
,
tv=z-x-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
and
|\Phi(t,v)|\lesssim\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
, we have

\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle+\langle z\rangle}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}+\frac{1}{\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle}+\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}.

If
|z|\geq t^{\frac{3}{2}}/2
, the right hand side of the previous inequality is bounded by
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}
. Otherwise
|z|\leq t^{\frac{3}{2}}/2
, so

|x|\geq\big|tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle\big|-|z|\geq t^{\frac{3}{2}}-t_{1}t-\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a+1}\langle t+1\rangle-\frac{1}{2}t^{\frac{3}{2}},

which allows to deduce (
29
). Finally, assume that
|\gamma|\geq N+2-p_{\infty}
, so that
|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}-1
. Here, we proceed as in
(c)
to get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t^{4}}{\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z|\rangle^{2}}\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})}\big\|\langle t+|y|\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|-3}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,y)\big\|_{L^{p}_{y}}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|g_{\beta}\big|^{p}\big(t,y-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}^{\frac{1}{p}}.

We bound the first factor by
t^{3/2}
using (
29
). The second one is controlled by
\Lambda_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-3+\frac{3}{p}}
through (

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
). For the last factor, we separate between the case
t\leq t_{0}
and
t\geq t_{0}
to bound it by
\langle t\rangle^{-3/p}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)
.

We finally deal with
\mathfrak{I}_{3}
. We then fix
1\leq|\gamma|\leq N
and
|\beta|\leq|\alpha|+1-|\gamma|
. Assume first that
|\gamma|\leq N-p_{\infty}
, so that, by (

L
∞

, sharp
),

\mathfrak{I}_{3}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\|g_{\beta}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Otherwise,
|\gamma|\geq N+1-p_{\infty}
and
|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}
. Using (

L
p

, sharp
) and
\int_{x}|g_{\beta}|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim|\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g]|^{p}
, we have

\mathfrak{I}_{3}\lesssim\big\|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi(t,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\lesssim\frac{\Lambda_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

As a consequence of the previous analysis, one can estimate
\partial_{t}g
and its derivatives up to order
N-1
. For global solutions, it will allow to obtain convergence to a limiting distribution function. From now on, we assume that
a=0
, that is
\|\Lambda_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(I)}\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
.

Corollary 3.10
.

Recall that
(\phi,\Phi)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(I,\mathbf{\Lambda})
, let
g\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular solution to
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=0
, and let
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. For any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
, we have

\forall\,t\in I,\qquad\big\|\partial_{t}g_{\alpha}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{\Lambda}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

Proof.

We use the expression of
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
, given by Lemma
2.4
, to rewrite
\partial_{t}g_{\alpha}
. As
|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi|(t,v)\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}
, it yields

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}g_{\alpha}|\lesssim\!\big|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(g_{\alpha}\big)\big|+\frac{t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,v)\Big||\nabla_{x}g_{\alpha}|+|\nabla_{x}\phi|(t,z)\bigg(\frac{\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle v\rangle}|\nabla_{x}g_{\alpha}|+|\nabla_{v}g_{\alpha}|\bigg)+\log\langle t\rangle|\partial_{t}\Phi||\nabla_{x}g_{\alpha}|.

Using (

L
∞

, sharp
) to bound
\partial_{t}\Phi
, and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
), we get

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}g_{\alpha}|\!\lesssim\big|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(g_{\alpha}\big)\big|+\!\sum_{|\beta|\leq N}\!\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)+\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)-\Phi(t,v)\Big||g_{\beta}|+\mathbf{\Lambda}\frac{\log\!\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}|g_{\beta}|.

We now observe that bound derived for (

I
1

1

) applies in the case
|\gamma|=0
. Thus, using also (

L
∞

, sharp
), we get

\big\|\partial_{t}g_{\alpha}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(g_{\alpha}\big)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\mathbf{\Lambda}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t).

It remains to apply Proposition
3.8
.

We will combine the last estimate with the next result to obtain scattering.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
T\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{g}\colon[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function satisfying
\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\in L^{1}_{t}
and
\mathfrak{g}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\in L^{p}_{x,v}
. Then, there exists
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
such that

\forall\,t\in[T,+\infty[,\qquad\big\|\mathfrak{g}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\int_{\tau=t}^{+\infty}\big\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Proof.

By the Minkowski integral inequality, we have, for all
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq T
,

\big\|\mathfrak{g}(t_{2},\cdot,\cdot)-\mathfrak{g}(t_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}=\bigg\|\int_{\tau=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\int_{\tau=t_{1}}^{+\infty}\big\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

As the right hand side goes to
0
as
t_{1}\to+\infty
, the map
t\mapsto g(t,\cdot,\cdot)
is Cauchy in
L^{p}_{x,v}
.

3.3  Convergence of characteristics and consequences

Let
T\geq 0
and assume, throughout this section, that
I=[T,+\infty[
. Recall that
(\phi,\Phi)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(I,\mathbf{\Lambda})
, for some constant
\mathbf{\Lambda}
. We will be interested in the asymptotic properties of the characteristics associated with the transport operator

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}=\partial_{t}+a_{x}(t,x,v)\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,z(t,x,v)\big)\cdot\nabla_{v},

where

a_{x}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,v)\Big)+\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(t,z)-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\Phi.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
t,\,t_{0}\geq T
and
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. We denote by
\varphi
the characteristic flow of
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
,

\varphi_{t,t_{0}}(x,v)\coloneqq\big(\mathbf{X}(t,t_{0},x,v),\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},x,v)\big),

where
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{V})(t_{0},t_{0},x,v)=(x,v)
and, using the dot to denote differentiation with respect to the first variable
t
,

\displaystyle\dot{\mathbf{X}}(t,t_{0},x,v)

\displaystyle=a_{x}\big(t,\mathbf{X}(t,t_{0},x,v),\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},x,v)\big),

\displaystyle\dot{\mathbf{V}}(t,t_{0},x,v)

\displaystyle=-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,z(t,\mathbf{X}(t,t_{0},x,v),\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},x,v))\big).

We start by proving that the characteristics converge.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
t_{0}\geq T
and
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Then, there exists
C[\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that, for all
t\geq T
,

\big|\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},x,v)-v\big|\leq C,\qquad\qquad\big|\mathbf{X}(t,t_{0},x,v)\big|\leq\langle x\rangle e^{C}.

Moreover, there exists a continuous map
\varphi_{\infty,t_{0}}(x,v)=(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t_{0},x,v)
such that, for all
t\geq T
,

\big|\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},x,v)-\mathbf{V}(\infty,t_{0},x,v)\big|\leq C\langle t\rangle^{-1},\qquad\qquad\big|\mathbf{X}(t,t_{0},x,v)-\mathbf{X}(\infty,t_{0},x,v)\big|\leq\langle x\rangle\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}Ce^{C}.

Proof.

Recall from (
27
) that

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(|v|)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2},\qquad\qquad\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(|x|)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\langle x\rangle.

It remains to apply Duhamel’s principle and, for
\mathbf{X}
, the Grönwall lemma. The convergences follow.

We now show the convergence of the flow, as
t_{0}\to+\infty
.

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
t\geq T
. There exists
\varphi_{t,\infty}(x,v)\in C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
such that

(I)

For any compact subset
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have
\|\varphi_{t,t_{0}}-\varphi_{t,\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\lesssim C\langle t_{0}\rangle^{-1/3}
, where
C[K,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
.

(II)

For any compact subset
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, there exists a compact
K^{\prime}
such that
\varphi_{t,t_{0}}^{-1}(K)\subset K^{\prime}
for all
t_{0}\in[T,+\infty]
.

(III)

\varphi_{t,\infty}
is a homeomorphism, and
\varphi_{t,\infty}^{-1}=\varphi_{\infty,t}
. Besides,
\varphi_{t,\infty}\in W^{1,\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, and
\det(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{t,\infty})\equiv 1
.

Proof.

Let us fix
t
and introduce
h(t_{0},x,v)\coloneqq\varphi_{t,t_{0}}(x,v)
, so that
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(h)=0
. Note now that for
n\coloneqq(-N-3,-N-3)
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h](t)<+\infty
. Recall from Definition
3.7
the notation
h_{\alpha}
. As a consequence, by applying the energy inequality of Proposition
2.22
, to
h_{\alpha}
for any
|\alpha|\leq N
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h](t_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h](t)\lesssim\bigg|\int_{\tau=t}^{t_{0}}\big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}(h_{\alpha})(\tau,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg|\lesssim\bigg|\int_{\tau=t}^{t_{0}}\frac{1}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h](\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg|,

where, in the last step, we used Proposition
3.8
. Boundedness follows from the Grönwall inequality. Then, Corollary
3.10
, together with Lemma
3.11
, yields the existence of
\varphi_{t,\infty}
such that

\sup_{t_{0}\geq T}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[h](t_{0})<+\infty,\qquad\qquad\lim_{t_{0}\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[\varphi_{t,t_{0}}-\varphi_{t,\infty}\big]=0.

Unfortunately, if
N
or
p
are not sufficiently large, we cannot conclude the proof without additional estimates. We claim now that, for any
|\alpha|\leq 1
,

\forall\,(\tau,x,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\quad\big|\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(h_{\alpha})\big|(\tau,x,v)\leq\mathbf{C}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{4}{3}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 1}\big|h_{\beta}\big|(\tau,x,v),\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0.

For this, we proceed as in the proof of Proposition
3.8
.

•

We bound
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(h_{\alpha})
using first Corollary
2.21
, applied with
|\alpha|=1
, and (
27
).

•

Then, in any term given by Corollary
2.21
, we can estimate the force field and the coefficients
\Phi
pointwise through (

Φ

, bound
), (

L
∞

, sharp
) and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
), exactly as in the proof of Proposition
3.8
.

Hence, we obtain from Duhamel’s principle and the Grönwall inequality, that

\forall\,t,\,\tau\geq T,\quad\big\|h_{\alpha}\big\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\mathbf{C},\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0.

Proceeding as in Corollary
3.10
, we get

\forall\,t,\,\tau\geq T,\quad\big\|\partial_{\tau}\langle x\rangle^{-N-3}\langle v\rangle^{-N-3}\varphi_{t,\tau}(x,v)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\mathbf{C}\langle\tau\rangle^{-\frac{4}{3}},\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0,

which allows us to obtain
(I)
and that
(x,v)\mapsto\varphi_{t,\infty}(x,v)
is continuous. As
\varphi_{t,t_{0}}\circ\varphi_{t_{0},t}=\varphi_{t_{0},t}\circ\varphi_{t,t_{0}}=\mathrm{id}
for all
t_{0}\geq T
, and that both
\varphi_{t,t_{0}}
and
\varphi_{t_{0},t}
are continuous for all
T\leq t_{0}\leq+\infty
, we get the first part of
(III)
. The Banach-Alaoglu theorem yields
\varphi_{t,\infty}\in W^{1,\infty}_{(x,v),\mathrm{loc}}
. To conclude the proof of
(III)
, we combine

\forall\,t_{0}\in[T,+\infty[,\quad\det\big(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{t,t_{0}}\big)\equiv 1,\qquad\qquad\forall\,T\leq t_{0}\leq+\infty,\quad\big\|\nabla_{x,v}\,\varphi_{t,t_{0}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{\Lambda},K],

for any compact
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, with the strong convergence in
L^{p}_{(x,v),\mathrm{loc}}
,
\varphi_{t,t_{0}}\to\varphi_{t,\infty}
, as
t_{0}\to+\infty
. Finally,
(II)
follows from Lemma
3.13
.

We are now able to show that sufficiently regular solutions to
\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(f)=0
converge strongly in
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
, along suitable corrections to the characteristics of the free transport operator.

Corollary 3.15
.

Assume that
g
satisfies
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=0
, and that
\sup_{t\geq T}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)<+\infty
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Then, there exists a function
f_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big](t)=0.

Moreover, we have for all
t\geq T
,

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\frac{\mathbf{\Lambda}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}}\sup_{\tau\geq T}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](\tau).

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{B}\coloneqq\sup_{t\geq T}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)
. According to Duhamel’s principle, we have, for
|\alpha|\leq N
,

g_{\alpha}(t,x,v)=g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,t}(x,v)+H_{\alpha}(t,x,v),\qquad\qquad H_{\alpha}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\int_{\tau=T}^{t}\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g_{\alpha})(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{\tau,t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Note now that
H_{\alpha}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
converges in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
as
t\to+\infty
. Indeed, one can show that it is a Cauchy sequence from the Minkowski integral inequality, and

\int_{\tau=t}^{+\infty}\Big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g_{\alpha})(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{\tau,t}(x,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\tau=\int_{\tau=t}^{+\infty}\Big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g_{\alpha})(\tau,x,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\frac{C[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}}\mathbf{B},

where we used first that
(x,v)\mapsto\varphi_{\tau,t}(x,v)
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism of Jacobian determinant equal to
1
, and then Proposition
3.8
. For the first term, let
\epsilon>0
and consider
h\in C^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
such that
\|h-g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\leq\epsilon
. By
(III)
,
\varphi_{\tau,\infty}
is a Lipschitz volume-preserving homeomorphism, so

\big\|g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,t}-g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,\infty}\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\leq 2\big\|g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)-h\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\big\|h(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,t}-h(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,\infty}\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}.

We bound the second term using
(I)
–
(II)
and the mean value theorem. As a consequence, denoting the limit of
H_{\alpha}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
by
H_{\alpha}^{\infty}
, we finally get

\big\|g_{\alpha}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-g_{\alpha}(T,\cdot,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{T,\infty}-H^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\leq 2\epsilon+C\langle t\rangle^{-1/3},\qquad C\big[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{B},\|\nabla h\|_{L^{\infty}},\mathrm{supp}\,h\big]>0.

(30)

The convergence with a rate for the weaker norm follows from Corollary
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
.

We can finally prove well-posedness for the asymptotic Cauchy problem associated with
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
f_{\infty}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Then, there exists a unique
g\in L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\big)
such that

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(g)=0,\qquad\qquad g(t,\cdot,\cdot)\to f_{\infty}\quad\text{as $t\to+\infty$}.

(ACpb)

Moreover, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, there exists
\mathbf{C}_{0}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\quad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]e^{\mathbf{C}_{0}\mathbf{\Lambda}\langle T\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}}}.

Proof.

Let
g\in L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\big)
be a weak solution to (
ACpb
). Then, we write

g(t,x,v)=g\big(t_{0},\varphi_{t_{0},t}(x,v)\big)-g\big(t_{0},\varphi_{\infty,t}(x,v)\big)+g\big(t_{0},\varphi_{\infty,t}(x,v)\big).

By assumption, the last term on the right hand side converges to
f_{\infty}\big(\varphi_{\infty,t}(x,v)\big)
in
L^{p}_{x,v}
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
. In view of Lemma
3.13
and Proposition
3.14
, we can argue as in the proof of Corollary
3.15
, when we proved that
g_{\alpha}(T,\varphi_{T,t})\to g_{\alpha}(T,\varphi_{T,\infty})
as
t\to+\infty
in
L^{p}_{x,v}
, to get

\big\|g\big(t_{0},\varphi_{t_{0},t}(x,v)\big)-g\big(t_{0},\varphi_{\infty,t}(x,v)\big)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\to 0\qquad\text{as $t_{0}\to+\infty$.}

We then deduce, by passing to the limit
t_{0}\to+\infty
, that
g(t,\cdot,\cdot)=f_{\infty}\circ\varphi_{\infty,t}
. Existence holds since Proposition
3.14
ensures that
f_{\infty}\circ\varphi_{\infty,t}
is a weak solution to (
ACpb
) with the stated regularity.

Finally, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
, we conclude by standard arguments as follows.

•

Assume first that
f_{\infty}
is compactly supported, so that, by Lemma
3.13
, the same holds for
g_{k}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\coloneqq f_{\infty}\circ\varphi_{k,t}
. In particular,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g_{k}](t)<+\infty
for all
t\geq T
. We can then apply the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
to
[g_{k}]_{\alpha}
, for any
|\alpha|\leq N
, and Proposition
3.8
, to get

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g_{k}](\tau)-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]\leq p\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\bigg|\int_{t=k}^{\tau}\Big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big([g_{k}]_{\alpha}\big)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\leq\bigg|\int_{t=k}^{\tau}pC_{0}\frac{\mathbf{\Lambda}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g_{k}](\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg|.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality yields a bound that is uniform in
k
. The Banach-Alaoglu theorem finally provides the desired energy bound for
g
.

•

Otherwise, let
(\chi_{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a family of cutoff functions such that
\chi_{k}(x,v)=1
for
|x|+|v|\leq k
and
\chi(x,v)=0
for
|x|+|v|\geq 2k
. Then, we apply the previous result to
g_{k}
, the unique solution to (
ACpb
) arising from the asymptotic data
f_{\infty}\chi_{k}
. It remains to pass to the limit
k\to+\infty
.

3.4  Existence of a solution to (
VP
) with asymptotic data

We now apply the results of this section to construct a solution
f
to the Vlasov–Poisson system (
VP
) corresponding to the scattering data
f_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}
, thereby proving the second part of Theorem
1.3
. More precisely, we work in the coordinate system
(t,z,v)
, where

z(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,\qquad\qquad\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}f_{\infty}(x,\cdot)\mathrm{d}x.

We then show that the asymptotic Cauchy problem associated with (
VP
),

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g)=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}g(t,x,x)\to f_{\infty}(x,v),

(31)

where the expression of
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}
is given in Lemma
2.4
, admits a unique solution belonging to
L^{\infty}([T,+\infty[,\mathcal{B})
, for some
T\geq 0
. The function
f
given by
f(t,x,v)=g(t,x-tv-\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log(t),v)
will then be a solution to (
VP
), that is
\mathbf{T}_{\phi[f]}(f)=0
.

We start by defining a sequence of approximate solutions as follows.

•

g_{0}
is the constant in time solution
g_{0}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f_{\infty}(x,v)
.

•

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
g_{k+1}
as the the unique solution to the asymptotic Cauchy problem

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f_{k}]}(g_{k+1})=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}g_{k+1}(t,x,v)=f_{\infty}(x,v),

where
f_{k}\big(t,z(t,x,v),v\big)=g_{k}(t,x,v)
, so that
\mathbf{T}_{\phi[f_{k}]}(f_{k+1})=0
. We recall that
\phi[f_{k}]=\nabla\Delta^{-1}\rho[f_{k}]
.

We start by proving boundedness for the sequence
(g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
T\geq 0
,
\varepsilon\geq 0
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]=\varepsilon
, and let
C[N,p,\varepsilon]>0
be a constant to be chosen sufficiently large. Then, there exist
T_{0}[N,p,\varepsilon]\geq 0
and
\varepsilon_{0}[N,p]>0
, such that the following holds. If
T\geq T_{0}
, or if
T=0
and
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then, for any
k\geq 0
,

(A)

The function
g_{k}
is well-defined and satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g_{k}](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
for all
t\geq T
,

(B)

The potential
\phi[f_{k}]
and
\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
satisfy
(\phi[f_{k}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}])\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([T,+\infty[,C\varepsilon\big)
.

Proof.

We perform an induction. As
g_{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)=f_{\infty}
and
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]=\varepsilon
,
(A)
holds for
k=0
. Next, if
(A)
holds for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, then
(B)
is satisfied at order
k
by Corollary
3.5
. Finally, consider
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
(B)
holds. Then, by applying Proposition
3.16
, we obtain that
g_{k}
is well-defined and, for all
t\geq T
,

\mathbb{E}_{N}[g_{k+1}](t)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]e^{\mathbf{C}_{0}\varepsilon\langle T\rangle^{-1/3}},\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}_{0}[N,p,\varepsilon]>0.

We then bound the right hand side by
2\varepsilon
by either choosing
T_{0}
sufficiently large compared to
\mathbf{C}_{0}\varepsilon
, or, if
T_{0}=0
, by choosing
\varepsilon
sufficiently small compared to
\mathbf{C}_{0}[N,p,1]
.

We are now able to show that the sequence of approximate solutions
(g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges, as
k\to+\infty
. We recall from Definition
1.1
the expression of the energy norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(n_{x},n_{v})}[\cdot]
, and that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(7,7)}[\cdot]
.

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
f_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\varepsilon\geq 0
be such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]=\varepsilon
. Then, if
T\geq T_{0}
, or if
T=0
and
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, there exists a solution
g
to (
31
), which satisfies

•

\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
for all
t\geq T
, and
(\phi[f],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}])\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([T,+\infty[,C\varepsilon\big)
with
C[N,p]>0
,

•

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\varepsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}}
for all
t\geq T
, and
\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\to 0
, as
t\to+\infty
.

Proof.

Recall that
(g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in the norm
\sup_{t\geq T}\mathbb{E}_{N}[\cdot]
by
(A)
. Moreover, using
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f_{k}]}(g_{k+1})=0
, together with (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
) and (

L
∞

, sharp
), we obtain that
\sup_{t\geq T}\|\partial_{t}g_{k}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}
is bounded uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{N}
. By the Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness lemma and a diagonal extraction argument, there exists a subsequence
(g_{k_{i}})
such that,

\forall\,T_{1}\in[T,+\infty[,\qquad\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sup_{T\leq t\leq T_{1}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{(6,6)}\big[g-g_{k_{i}}\big](t)=0.

Then, we apply Banach-Alaoglu theorem to get, from
(A)
, that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
for all
t\geq T
. Moreover, one can either use again the Banach-Alaoglu theorem and
(B)
, or use Proposition
2.13
and Corollary
2.17
, to obtain
(\phi[f],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}])\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([T,+\infty[,C\epsilon\big)
. Convergence in this norm allows us to pass to the limit in the equation
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f_{n-1}]}(g_{n})=0
, so that
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g)=0
. Finally, the convergence statements follow from Corollary
3.15
.

We will conclude the proof of the converse statement in Theorem
1.3
, that is show uniqueness for the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
31
), in Corollary
4.6
below. We finish by a propagation result for higher weighted Sobolev norms.

Corollary 3.19
.

Assume that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Then, there exists
B[N,p,n,\varepsilon]>0
such that,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq B\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]\leq B\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Moreover, we have
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}]\to 0
as
t\to+\infty
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
3.16
and Corollary
3.15
.

3.5  Small data solutions

We now establish the first part of Theorem
1.3
, that is global existence and modified scattering for the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system. Let
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
f
be a solution to (
VP
) such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f](0)=\varepsilon
. We define further

\Phi_{f}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv),\qquad\qquad g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big),

so that
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g)=0
. We consider
T\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
, the maximal time such that

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([0,T[,C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon\big),

(BA1)

for some constant
C_{\mathrm{boot}}[N,p]>0
which will be fixed sufficiently large later. By local well-posedness,
T>0
. Let us now improve, if
\varepsilon
is small enough, the bootstrap assumption (
BA1
). Proposition
3.8
, together with the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
, applied to
g_{\alpha}
for any
|\alpha|\leq N
, yields

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](\tau)-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](0)\leq p\int_{t=0}^{\tau}C_{0}[N,p,n,C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon]\frac{C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\mathrm{d}t,

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](0)<+\infty
. By the Grönwall inequality, we obtain, if
\varepsilon
is small enough, that

\forall\,t\in[0,T[,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](\tau)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](0)e^{4pC_{0}C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon}.

(32)

Assume now that
\varepsilon
is small enough so that
C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon\leq 1
. In particular,
C_{0}
does not depend on
(C_{\mathrm{boot}},\varepsilon)
. Applying (
32
) to
n=(7,7)
, we obtain, if
\varepsilon
is small enough, that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
for all
t\in[0,T[
. Then, by applying Corollary
3.3
for
\Phi=\Phi_{f}
, and using again
C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon\leq 1
, we get

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([0,T[,\mathbf{C}\varepsilon\big),\qquad\mathbf{C}[N,p]>0.

We then choose
C_{\mathrm{boot}}=2\mathbf{C}
. This improves (
BA1
) and implies that
T=+\infty
. Moreover, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
and (
32
) hold for all
t\geq 0
. We finally show
(B)
–
(C)
in Theorem
1.3
. Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](0)<+\infty
.

•

According to Corollary
3.15
, there exists
f_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}]\to 0
, as
t\to+\infty
. Note in particular that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]\leq 2\varepsilon
. However, this is not enough for our purposes since we would like to prove that
f
converges along slightly different corrections to the linear characteristics.

•

In this step, we prove the existence of an effective force field. We recall that
\Phi_{f}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
, and that
\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]
satisfies
\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]=\int_{x}f_{\infty}(x,\cdot),\mathrm{d}x
. Then, combining Corollary
2.17
, the bound on
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]
, and the previous step, we get the following estimates. For all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\langle v\rangle\Big(\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f}(t,v)-\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\frac{\log^{2N+7}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle},\qquad\qquad|\kappa|\leq N-p_{\infty}.

(33)

•

Finally, consider the function
G
given by

G(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log(t),v\big)=g\big(t,x+\lambda\big[\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)-\Phi_{f}(t,v)\big]\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

Then, by the mean value theorem in
x
and (
33
), we have
G(t,\cdot,\cdot)\to f_{\infty}
in
L^{p}_{x,v}
as
t\to+\infty
. Using
\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi_{f}](t)\lesssim\varepsilon
and
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\varepsilon
for all
t\geq 0
, and arguing exactly as in the proof of Corollary
3.5
, we obtain

\big(\phi[f],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},C\varepsilon\big),\qquad\qquad C[N,p]>0.

Since
G\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+},L^{p}_{x,v})
, we apply Proposition
3.16
to bound
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[G]
in terms of
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]
, which yields
(B)
in Theorem
1.3
. Corollary
3.15
then gives the scattering result
(C)
. Finally, this also proves Remark
1.4
.

4
Global existence and modified scattering for perturbations of dispersive solutions

We aim to construct a solution
f
to the Vlasov-Poisson system, with initial data close to the ones of a global and dispersive solution
\mathbf{f}
to (
VP
). We will then study the function
g-\mathbf{g}
, with

g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,z_{f}(t,x,v),v\big),\qquad\qquad\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathbf{f}\big(t,z_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v),v\big),

where the coordinates
z_{f}
and
z_{\mathbf{f}}
are given by

z_{f}(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,\qquad\qquad z_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,

and the coefficients
\Phi_{f}
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
will be of one of the following two forms.

(
i
)

For the forward problem, we will use
\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathfrak{f}](t,tv)
.

(
ii
)

For the backward problem, we will set
\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}(v)\coloneqq\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}](v)
, for
\mathfrak{f}(t,z_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,x,v),v)\to\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}(x,v)
as
t\to+\infty
.

For all this section, we fix an order of regularity
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
,
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
, a constant
\mathbf{\Lambda}>0
, and a global solution
\mathbf{f}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
to (
VP
). Assume that, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big),

(H
\mathbf{f}
)

where
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
is either given by
(i)
or by
(ii)
.

4.1  Preparatories

We complement here the results of Section
2
. We fix an interval of times
I\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and assume that, in addition to (
H

f

), the following estimates hold,

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big(I,2\mathbf{\Lambda}\big),\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big(I,\omega\big),

(H
f
)

for a nonnegative function
\omega\colon t\mapsto\omega_{t}
. We start by computing
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g-\mathbf{g})
.

Lemma 4.1
.

The function
g-\mathbf{g}
satisfies

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}\big(g-\mathbf{g}\big)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}-\lambda t\big(\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\big)\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle+\lambda\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})+\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{g}+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,z_{f})\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\nabla_{v}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\cdot\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathbf{f}](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{g}.

Proof.

We use that
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g)=\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[\mathbf{f}]}(\mathbf{g})=0
, and that
\phi[\cdot]
as well as
\psi\mapsto\Phi_{\psi}
are linear.

We now show a result similar to Corollary
2.21
.

Corollary 4.2
.

For any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
, we can bound
\langle x\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\alpha_{x}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g-\mathbf{g})\big|
by a linear combination of the terms

\displaystyle\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big|,\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|+1,\quad|\gamma|\leq|\alpha|,

\displaystyle\log\langle t\rangle\Big|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big|,

\displaystyle|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|+1,\quad|\gamma|\leq|\alpha|,

and

\displaystyle\log^{N}\langle t+1\rangle\Big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}|\Big)\cdot\Big|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big|,

\displaystyle\log^{N}\langle t+1\rangle\Big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}|\Big)\cdot\Big|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|+1}\Big(\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{f})-\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\Big)\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big|,

\displaystyle\log^{N}\langle t+1\rangle\Big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}|\Big)\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big|\cdot\Big|(t+|z_{f}|)^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,z_{f})\Big|\cdot\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big|,

where
|\beta|\geq 1
,
|\xi|+|\kappa|+|\gamma|+\big(|\beta|-1\big)\leq|\alpha|
, and
\mathfrak{f}\in\{f,\mathbf{f}\}
.

Proof.

We begin by stating a result analogous to Corollary
2.20
. Note first that for a function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\partial_{x^{i}}\big[\psi(z_{f})\big]=\partial_{x^{i}}\psi(z_{f}),\qquad\qquad\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\psi(z_{f})\big]=t\partial_{x^{i}}\psi(z_{f})+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi_{f}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi(z_{f}),

and

\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\psi(z_{f})-\psi(z_{\mathbf{f}})\big]=t\big(\partial_{x^{i}}\psi(z_{f})-\partial_{x^{i}}\psi(z_{\mathbf{f}})\big)+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi(z_{f})+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\cdot\big(\nabla_{x}\psi(z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\psi(z_{\mathbf{f}})\big).

We recall from Definition
2.18
the notation
P_{r,q}(\Phi_{f})
. We use the obvious extension
P_{r,q}(\Phi_{f},\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})
for products involving both
\Phi_{f}
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
. Hence, by Lemma
4.1
and an induction, we can write
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g-\mathbf{g})
as a linear combination of the terms

\displaystyle\frac{t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big(\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g},\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v},

\displaystyle\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g},

\displaystyle\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v},

and, with the convention that
\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}=\partial_{x^{i}}
if
\delta=1
and
\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}=\partial_{v^{i}}
for
\delta=0
,

\displaystyle\log^{r}\langle t\rangle P_{r,q}(\Phi_{f},\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})\cdot t^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-q-r}\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})\cdot\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g},\qquad\delta\leq r\leq|\gamma|+1,

(34)

\displaystyle\log^{r}\langle t\rangle P_{r,q}(\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})\cdot t^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-q-r}\big(\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{f})-\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\big)\cdot\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g},\qquad r\leq|\gamma|,

(35)

\displaystyle\log^{r+1}\langle t\rangle P_{r,q}(\Phi_{f},\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})\partial_{v^{k}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\cdot t^{\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-|\kappa|-q-r-1}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,z_{f})\cdot\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}\mathbf{g},\qquad r\leq|\gamma|,

(36)

where
q+|\kappa|+|\gamma|+|\beta|=|\alpha|
,
|\beta_{x}|\leq|\alpha_{x}|
,
|\beta_{v}|\leq|\alpha_{v}|
. Moreover, the exponent of
t
is nonnegative. In other words, in (
34
)–(
35
) and (
36
), we respectively have

\displaystyle\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-q-r

\displaystyle=|\gamma|+|\beta_{x}|-|\alpha_{x}|+\delta-r\geq 0,

(37)

\displaystyle\delta+|\alpha_{v}|-|\beta_{v}|-|\kappa|-q-r-1

\displaystyle=|\gamma|+|\beta_{x}|-|\alpha_{x}|+\delta-r-1\geq 0.

(38)

As for Corollary
2.21
, the first two family of terms can be easily handled. For the last three ones, we proceed as for the terms of (
type–

2

) in the proof of Corollary
2.21
. Recall first that
\Phi_{f}
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
satisfy (

Φ

, bound
) for
\Lambda_{t}=2\mathbf{\Lambda}
. Since
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
, the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, together with (
H

f

)–(
H

f

), implies that, if
r\geq 1
, there exists
|\xi|\leq q
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\{f,\mathbf{f}\}
such that

\big|P_{r,q}(\Phi_{f},\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})\big|(t,v)\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}^{r-1}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}\big|(t,v).

(39)

Then, we write
\partial_{x^{i}}^{\delta}\partial_{v^{i}}^{1-\delta}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta_{v}}=\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}^{\prime}}\partial_{v}^{\beta^{\prime}_{v}}
and we consider two cases.

•

If
|\beta_{x}^{\prime}|=|\alpha_{x}|+1
, which occurs if
|\beta_{x}|=|\alpha_{x}|
and
\delta=1
. Then, in view of (
37
)–(
38
), we focus on

t^{|\gamma|+1-r}\langle x\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\alpha_{x}|}=\frac{\langle v\rangle}{\langle x\rangle}t^{|\gamma|+1-r}\langle x\rangle^{|\beta^{\prime}_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\beta^{\prime}_{x}|}.

Note then that for the terms (
34
), we have
t^{|\gamma|+1-r}\leq\langle t\rangle^{|\gamma|}
by (

) and
r\geq\delta
. We further absorb the weight
\langle v\rangle
through (
39
).

For the terms (

), since we have an extra factor
t^{-1}
, we need to show

, which follows from (

). The weight

is absorbed by the factor
\partial_{v^{k}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
.

Finally, for the terms (

), we have

since

. Then, we use

t\langle v\rangle\leq t+|tv|\leq t+|z_{f}|+|x|+\|\Phi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,v}}\log\langle t\rangle\lesssim\langle x\rangle\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle.

•

Otherwise,
\delta+|\beta_{x}|=|\beta^{\prime}_{x}|\leq|\alpha_{x}|
and we recall from (

) that
\langle x\rangle\lesssim\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle\,\langle v\rangle
, so that

t^{|\gamma|+|\beta_{x}|-|\alpha_{x}|+\delta-r}\langle x\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\alpha_{x}|}\lesssim t^{|\gamma|+|\beta_{x}^{\prime}|-|\alpha_{x}|-r}\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|-|\beta_{x}^{\prime}|}\langle x\rangle^{|\beta^{\prime}_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\beta^{\prime}_{x}|}.

We then get the desired bounds by using (
37
)–(
38
), to deal with small times
0\leq t\leq 1
, and
t\leq\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle
.

The next corollary will be useful in the perspective of performing energy estimates. We recall from Definition
3.7
the notation
g_{\beta}
.

Corollary 4.3
.

There exists
\mathfrak{C}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that, for any
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N-1
,

\forall\,t\in I,\qquad\qquad\Big\|\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g-\mathbf{g})(t,\cdot,\cdot)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\mathfrak{C}\frac{\omega_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}.

Proof.

In view of Corollary
4.2
, we are lead to bound the following five quantities,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big|\langle t\rangle^{2}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})-\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\Big|\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\gamma|+|\beta|=N,\quad|\gamma|\leq N-1,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\langle v\rangle\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\gamma|+|\beta|=N,\quad|\gamma|\leq N-1,

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\big(1+\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}\big|\big)\cdot\Big|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,z_{f})\Big|\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{4}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}|\big)\cdot\Big|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|+1}\big(\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{f})-\partial_{x^{k}}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\big)\Big|\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{5}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\big(1+|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}|\big)\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big|\cdot\Big|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,z_{f})\Big|\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

where
|\beta|\geq 1
,
|\xi|+|\kappa|+|\gamma|+\big(|\beta|-1\big)\leq N-1
and
\mathfrak{f}\in\{f,\mathbf{f}\}
.

By proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
3.8
for the analysis of
\mathfrak{I}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{2}
, and
\mathfrak{I}_{3}
, one can show

\mathfrak{K}_{1}\lesssim\omega_{t}\frac{\log\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t),\qquad\mathfrak{K}_{2}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\frac{\omega_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t),\qquad\mathfrak{K}_{3}\lesssim\frac{\omega_{t}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{9}{5}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t).

In particular, to estimate
\phi[f-\mathbf{f}]
and
\partial_{t}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
through (
H

f

) for
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{3}
, we note that either
|\gamma|\leq N-1-p_{\infty}
, or
|\gamma|\geq N-p_{\infty}
and
|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}
. Then, we conclude by using
\mathbf{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
.

For the analysis of
\mathfrak{K}_{4}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{5}
, we will use the following ingredients.

1.

If
|\xi|,\,|\kappa|,\,|\gamma|\leq N-1-p_{\infty}
, then

\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big|\lesssim\omega_{t},\qquad\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{f}\big|+\big|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big|\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{2+|\gamma|}\big|\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,z_{f})\big|\leq 2\mathbf{\Lambda}.\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\quad\square\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Recall the assumptions (
H

f

)–(
H

f

). The derivatives of
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
,

and

. For

).

according to the Sobolev embedding for Banach-valued functions

and

We start by studying

We consider now the case

,

•

Finally, if

, we have

and

. Then, in that case, we have

\mathfrak{K}_{5}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\omega_{t}\Big\|\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,z_{f})\big|\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Recall from (

f

) that we can estimate

in

,

, weak
). Then, proceeding exactly as in

Let us finally deal with
\mathfrak{K}_{4}
. Note that the fundamental theorem of calculus gives, since
z_{f}-z_{\mathbf{f}}=\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
,

\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})=\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\cdot\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big]\Big(t,z_{f}-\theta\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle\Big)\mathrm{d}\theta.

Let us use the notation
y_{\theta}(t,x,v)\coloneqq z_{f}-\theta\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
. As
\|\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\leq\omega_{t}\leq 3\mathbf{\Lambda}
, we have
t+|z_{f}|\leq 6\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\langle t+|y_{\theta}|\rangle
and

\langle t+|z_{f}|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\Big|\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{f})-\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,z_{\mathbf{f}})\Big|\lesssim\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\langle t+|y_{\theta}|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\Big|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,y_{\theta})\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta.

(40)

•

Consider first the case
|\xi|,\,|\gamma|\leq N-1-p_{\infty}
, so that
|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}|\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}
. We estimate pointwise the force field using (
40
) and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
), since
(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}})\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda})

\mathfrak{K}_{4}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\frac{\omega_{t}\mathbf{\Lambda}\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}\frac{\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}.

•

If

. We can then use again (

\mathfrak{K}_{4}\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}\frac{\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}+\frac{\omega_{t}\mathbf{\Lambda}\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathbf{\Lambda}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}\frac{\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}.

We finally study the case

, so that

) and the Minkowski integral inequality to get

\mathfrak{K}_{4}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\Big\|\langle t+|y_{\theta}|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,y_{\theta})\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}\theta,

where we recall

X(x)=y_{\theta}(t,x,v)
to get

\mathfrak{K}_{4}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\Big\|\langle t+|X|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla^{2}_{x}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\mathbf{f}\big](t,X)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{X})}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|\mathbf{g}_{\beta}\big|^{p}(t,Y_{\theta}(t,X,v),v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{X})}^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathrm{d}\theta,

where
Y_{\theta}(t,x,v)=x-tv-\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\big[(1-\theta)\Phi_{f}+\theta\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big](t,v)
. We may now proceed as in
(c)
, where
