was estimated, to obtain

\mathfrak{K}_{4}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{\Lambda}\rangle\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}\omega_{t}\log\langle t\rangle\langle t\rangle^{-2}.

Next, we will require a result similar to Lemma
2.6
.

Lemma 4.4
.

We have, for all
t\in I
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)-\mathbf{g}(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)\big](t)\lesssim\log^{2N+6}\langle t+1\rangle\,\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}](t).

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathbf{g}(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
. We follow the proof of Lemma
2.6
up to (
11
), with
\Phi=-\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
,
g=\mathbf{g}
, and
f_{\circ}=\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
. It yields, for any
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N-1
,

\bigg\|\hskip-0.39993pt\frac{\langle x\rangle^{7+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{7+N}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}}\hskip-0.39993pt\Big(\hskip-0.50003pt\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}(t,x,v)-\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big]\hskip-0.50003pt\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\hskip-0.50003pt\Big)\hskip-0.80002pt\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\!\lesssim\log^{b}\langle t+1\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi](t)\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[\mathbf{g}](t),

where
b=2N+5
. To conclude the proof, let us prove that

\bigg\|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{7+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{7+N}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\alpha_{x}|}}\Big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)-\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim\log^{b^{\prime}}\!\langle t+1\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi](t)\mathbb{E}_{N}[\mathbf{g}](t),

where
b^{\prime}=N+8
. Indeed, we first apply the fundamental theorem of calculus. It remains to use the Minkowski integral inequality,
\langle v\rangle|\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)|\leq\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}](t)
,
\langle x\rangle\lesssim\big\langle x+\theta\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle\big\rangle\log\langle t+1\rangle
for all
0\leq\theta\leq 1
and to perform the change of variables
y(x)=x+\theta\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
.

We conclude this subsection by proving the following result, which is a refinement of Proposition
2.11
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Recall that the assumptions (
H

f

)–(
H

f

) hold. Then, for any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
and all
t\geq 0
,

\Big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}+|\alpha|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho\big[f-\mathbf{f}\big](t,x)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g-\mathbf{g}\big](t)+\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{f}(t,x,v)\coloneqq x-tv-\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
, that we will often denote by
\mathcal{Z}_{f}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
be defined as

\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}(t,x,v)=\mathbf{g}\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)=\mathbf{f}\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

We start by applying the identities (
13
) and (
15
). Separating the cases
t+|x|\leq 1
and
t+|x|\geq 1
, it yields, for
h=g-\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
,

\displaystyle\langle t+|x|\rangle^{|\alpha|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho\big[f-\mathbf{f}\big]\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|+|\kappa_{\Omega}|+m\leq|\alpha|}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\Omega^{\kappa_{\Omega}}S^{m}h\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{f}(t,v)|}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\langle\mathcal{Z}_{f}\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)\big|\mathrm{d}v.

Then, as
N-1\geq 2p_{\infty}-1
, we can apply Lemma
2.10
to get, for any
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{1+|\xi|}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\xi}\Phi_{f}(t,v)|}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\langle\mathcal{Z}_{f}\rangle^{|\beta_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{|\beta_{v}|}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}h\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)\big|\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}](t)}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h](t).

Note now that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[h](t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)-\mathbf{g}\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+\log^{2N+6}(t)\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t),

(41)

where, in the last step, we used Lemma
4.4
. Next, we fix multi-indices
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|+|\kappa_{\Omega}|+m\leq|\alpha|
and we use the shorthand
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\Omega^{\kappa_{\Omega}}S^{m}
. Then, we remark

\big[\mathcal{D}h\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)=\big[\mathcal{D}g-\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)+\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)-\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)+\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)-\big[\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big).

Applying Lemma
2.10
, together with (
16
), one gets

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big[\mathcal{D}g-\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t).

For the second quantity to estimate, we introduce
\mathbf{h}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}
to lighten the notations. Note first that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)-\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)\mathrm{d}v=\log\langle t\rangle\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{h}\big(t,x-tv-\lambda\big[\Phi_{f}-\theta\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\theta.

If
t\geq 1
, we recall, for
\mathcal{Z}(t,x,v)=x-tv-\lambda\Phi(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle
, the identity

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}\mathbf{h}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{t}\Big(\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\big]+\lambda\log\langle t\rangle\partial_{v^{i}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)-\partial_{v^{i}}\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z},v\big)\Big).

(42)

Applying it for
\Phi=\Phi_{f}-\theta\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
, which satisfies
|\nabla_{v}\Phi(t,v)|\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}
, and performing integration by parts, yields

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)-\mathbf{h}\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p_{\infty}}\!\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t)}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\sum_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\!\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\mathbf{h}\big(t,x-tv-\lambda\big[\Phi_{f}-\theta\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\big|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\theta,

since
|\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}|(t,v)+|\nabla_{v}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}|(t,v)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{p_{\infty}}[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}](t)
by the Sobolev embedding
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Then, we bound
\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}
using (
16
). We finally obtain from Lemma
2.10
, together with the Minkowski inequality for integrals, that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)-\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t)}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[\mathbf{g}](t),

where we also used
p_{\infty}\leq N-1
. We conclude by using
\mathbb{E}_{N}[\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
. We finally give the main steps to estimate the last quantity.

•

Estimate
\big[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{g}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{f}},v\big)-\big[\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}\big]\big(t,\mathcal{Z}_{f},v\big)
by carrying out computations similar to those used in the proof of Lemma
2.6
.

•

Control the resulting terms using (
42
), integration by parts, and (
16
). Finally, apply Lemma
2.10
.

4.2  The backward problem

We finish here the construction of the wave operator
\mathscr{W}_{+}
, introduced in Definition
1.5
. For this, to complement Proposition
3.18
, we need to show uniqueness for the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
31
) associated to the Vlasov-Poisson system. Then, we study regularity properties of
\mathscr{W}_{+}
.

We fix, for all this section,
(f_{\infty},\mathbf{f}_{\infty})\in\mathcal{B}^{2}
. We assume that
g
and
\mathbf{g}
are two solutions to the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
31
), with respective scattering data
f_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{f}_{\infty}
, respectively defined on the time intervals
[T_{g},+\infty[
and
[T_{\mathbf{g}},+\infty[
, where
T_{g},\,T_{\mathbf{g}}\geq 0
, and such that

\sup_{t\geq T_{\mathbf{g}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t)=\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq T_{g}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbf{\Lambda},\,\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}\geq 0.

In this section, we then have

•

\Phi_{f}(t,v)=\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}(t,v)=\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[\mathbf{f}_{\infty}](v)
.

•

z_{f}(t,x,v)=x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(v)\log\langle t\rangle
and
z_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v)=x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}(v)\log\langle t\rangle
.

•

f
and
\mathbf{f}
, satisfying
g(t,x,v)=f(t,z_{f}(t,x,v),v)
and
\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)=\mathbf{f}(t,z_{\mathbf{f}}(t,x,v),v)
, are two solutions to (
VP
).

Note that in view of Proposition
2.12
,

\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi_{f}]\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}_{N}[\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}]\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}].

(43)

We can then apply the results of Section
2
for
\Phi=\Phi_{f}
and
\Phi=\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}
. We start by controlling
g-\mathbf{g}
.

Corollary 4.6
.

We have, for all
t\in[\max(T_{g},T_{\mathbf{g}}),+\infty[
,

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{1}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}_{1}\big[N,n,p,\max(\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0})\big]>0.

Proof.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
, and define
I\coloneqq[\min(T_{g},T_{\mathbf{g}}),+\infty[
. In view of applying Proposition
3.8
and Corollary
4.3
, let us prove that there exists a constant
C_{2}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]
such that

\big(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(I,C_{2}\mathbf{\Lambda}\big),\qquad\quad\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(I,C_{2}\mathbf{\Lambda}\big),\quad\qquad\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big(I,\omega\big),

where
\omega_{t}=C_{2}E_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+C_{2}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]
. For
\big(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big)
and
\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)
, this follows from Corollary
3.5
. For
(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}})
, we proceed as follows.

•

We note that
\partial_{t}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}=0
, and we obtain (

Φ

, bound
) by (
43
).

•

We obtain (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) from Proposition
4.5
and the elliptic estimates of Corollary
2.3
.

•

We derive (

L
∞

, sharp
)–(

L
p

, sharp
) by combining Corollary
2.17
with (
41
)–(
43
).

Recall from Definition
3.7
the notation
(g-\mathbf{g})_{\alpha}
, and apply the energy inequality of Proposition
2.22
to
(g-\mathbf{g})_{\alpha}
, for any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
. Using Proposition
3.8
and Corollary
4.3
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g-\mathbf{g}](\tau)-\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\int_{t=\tau}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}\big]\mathrm{d}t.

An application of the Grönwall inequality allows to conclude.

By applying the previous Corollary
4.6
with
f_{\infty}=\mathbf{f}_{\infty}
, we obtain uniqueness for the asymptotic Cauchy problem associated with the Vlasov-Poisson system in
L^{\infty}([T,+\infty[,\mathcal{B})
, for any sufficiently large
T\geq 0
. This concludes the construction of the modified wave operator. Next, we focus on the regularity properties of
\mathscr{W}_{+}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Assume that
\mathbf{f}
is a global solution to (
VP
) satisfying the hypotheses (
H

f

). Let
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0},\,\varepsilon\geq 0
, and let
f_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}
satisfy
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}
and
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]=\varepsilon
. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}]>0
such that, if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then the following hold:

•

The unique solution
f
to (
VP
) with scattering data
f_{\infty}
is global in time,

•

If, in addition,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
, then there exists
C[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}]>0
such that
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq C\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]
.

Proof.

Let
T\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
be the minimal time such that, for all
t\geq T
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{boot}},

(BA2)

for some constant
C_{\mathrm{boot}}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}]\geq\mathbf{\Lambda}
which will be fixed sufficiently large later. According to Proposition
3.18
, and since uniqueness holds, we have
T\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. More precisely, there exists
T_{0}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}]\geq 0
such that

\forall\,t\geq T_{0},\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq 2\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}.

(44)

Let us show that, if
\varepsilon
is small enough, then we can improve the bootstrap assumption (
BA2
). This will imply that
T=0
. Note first that Corollary
4.6
and (
BA2
) provide

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{1}[N,p,C_{\mathrm{boot}}]\varepsilon.

Moreover, the proof of Corollary
4.6
also provides
\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([T,+\infty[,\mathbf{C}_{1}\varepsilon\big)
, possibly after redefining
\mathbf{C}_{1}[N,p,C_{\mathrm{boot}}]
as a larger constant. As a consequence, and since (
H

f

) holds, if
\varepsilon
is small enough, we have

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([T,+\infty[,2\mathbf{\Lambda}\big).

In particular, using (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
), we observe that the assumptions of Proposition
A.1
are satisfied. We then obtain

\forall\,T\leq t\leq T_{0},\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]\cdot\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](T_{0})\log^{7+(15+2N)N}\langle T_{0}+1\rangle,

where
f_{\circ}(t,x,v)=f(t,x+tv,v)
. Using both Lemma
2.6
and Remark
2.7
, together with
\mathcal{E}[\Phi_{f}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\infty}]
, we get

\forall\,T\leq t\leq T_{0},\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq\widetilde{C}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}]\cdot\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](T_{0})\log^{21+(19+2N)N}\langle T_{0}+1\rangle.

In view of (
44
), it only remains to choose
C_{\mathrm{boot}}
larger than both
3\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}
and twice the right-hand side of the previous inequality. For the second part, the proof of Corollary
4.6
gives
(\phi[f],\Phi_{f})\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(R_{+},C_{2}[N,p,C_{\mathrm{boot}}]C_{\mathrm{boot}}\big)
. Proposition
3.8
, together with the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
, yields boundedness of
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g]
.

As a consequence,
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is defined on an open subset
\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\subset\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}
. Furthermore, since (
BA2
) holds globally in time, it follows from Corollary
4.6
that
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is locally Lipschitz with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[\cdot]
.

4.3  The forward problem

In this section, we associate to a function
\mathfrak{f}\colon[0,T[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
the coefficient
\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}
, the coordinate
z_{\mathfrak{f}}
, and the function
\mathfrak{g}\colon[0,T[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[\mathfrak{f}](t,tv),\qquad\quad z_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,\qquad\quad\mathfrak{g}(t,x,v)=\mathfrak{f}\big(t,z_{\mathfrak{f}}(t,x,v),v\big).

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\mathbf{f}
be a global solution to (
VP
) satisfying the assumptions (
H

f

), namely
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\big(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big)
, for some
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
. Let
f
be another solution to (
VP
) such that

\mathbb{E}_{N}[f](0)\leq 5\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f-\mathbf{f}](0)=\varepsilon,\qquad\varepsilon\geq 0.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that, if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then
f
is global in time. Moreover, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f](0)<+\infty
, then there exist constants
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}^{n}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
and
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{Lip}}^{n}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
, such that

\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq\mathbf{C}^{n}_{\mathrm{top}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f](0),\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{Lip}}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[f-\mathbf{f}](0).

Remark 4.9
.

The analysis carried out in Section
4.3.3
below will show that
\mathbf{f}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)
. Conversely, if there exists
\mathbf{f}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}_{N}^{n}
such that
\mathbf{f}(0,\cdot,\cdot)=\mathscr{W}_{+}(\mathbf{f}_{\infty})
, then
\mathbf{f}
satisfies (
H

f

) for some
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
. Indeed, we have

\mathcal{E}_{N}\big[\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]\big]<+\infty,\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[\mathbf{G}\big](t)<+\infty,\quad\mathbf{G}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathbf{f}\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

Corollary
3.3
then implies that
\big(\phi[\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big)
, for some
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
. The uniform bound on
\mathbb{E}_{N}[\mathbf{f}]
then follows from Proposition
3.8
, together with Proposition
2.22
and Grönwall’s inequality.

In addition, several important estimates for
f
will be derived throughout the proof of Proposition
4.8
. To this end, we introduce a time
T_{0}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]\geq 0
, which will be chosen sufficiently large below.

4.3.1 Estimates for a compact domain of times

We start by studying the solution on the time interval
[0,T_{0}]
. Recall the notation
\mathfrak{f}_{\circ}(t,x,v)=\mathfrak{f}(t,x+tv,v)
. By Cauchy stability, there exist
\varepsilon_{1}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},T_{0}]>0
and
C_{T_{0}}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},T_{0}]\geq 1
such that, if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, then
1
1

1

One may show that
C_{T_{0}}
grows only polynomially in
T_{0}
, rather than exponentially as predicted by a standard Grönwall argument.

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ}](t)\leq C_{T_{0}}\varepsilon.

(45)

We now need to estimate
g
in order to prepare the analysis for
t\geq T_{0}
. Recall that
N-1\geq 2p_{\infty}-1
, and apply Corollary
3.3
with
\Phi=0
. It yields

\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([0,T_{0}],\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}\varepsilon\big),

for some
\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},T_{0}]>0
. Let us finally show that, up to redefining
\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}
to be a larger constant,

\forall\,t\in[0,T_{0}],\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}\varepsilon.

For this, we write

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[(f_{\circ}-\mathbf{f}_{\circ})(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)\big]+\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[\mathbf{g}(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)-\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)\big].

We handle the first term using Remark
2.7
, applied with
\Phi=\Phi_{f}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}\varepsilon+\mathbf{\Lambda}
. We deal with the second one by combining Lemma
4.4
with the bound
\mathcal{E}_{N-1}[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}\varepsilon
.

4.3.2 Estimates for large times

Let
T\in[T_{0},+\infty]
be the maximal time such that

\displaystyle\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([0,T[,C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon\big),

(BA3)

where
C_{\mathrm{boot}}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},T_{0}]\geq\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}
is a constant which will be fixed below.

Lemma 4.10
.

If
\varepsilon
is small enough, there exists
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that

\sup_{0\leq t<T}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)\leq\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}},\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([0,T[,\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}\big).

Proof.

In view of (
H

f

) and the bootstrap assumption (
BA3
), one has, for
\varepsilon
small enough,

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([0,T[,2\mathbf{\Lambda}\big).

(46)

As a consequence, we control
f_{\circ}
through Proposition
A.1
proved below. Applied with
N_{0}=N
and
a=0
, it yields

\forall\,t\in[0,T[,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](t)\lesssim\log^{7+(15+2N)N}\langle t+1\rangle.

Applying Corollary
3.3
with
\Phi=0
yields

\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big([0,T[,C\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}]\big),\qquad C[N,p]>0.

One then obtains boundedness for
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g]
by combining the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
, applied to
g_{\alpha}
for any
|\alpha|\leq N
, Proposition
3.8
, and the Grönwall inequality. The proof is completed by applying Corollary
3.3
with

\Phi=\Phi_{f}
and
a=7+(15+2N)N
.

We are now able to bound
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g-\mathbf{g}]
. Note that if
N-1\geq 2p_{\infty}
, we can simply use (
46
) instead of Lemma
4.10
.

Lemma 4.11
.

There exists
T_{1}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]\geq 0
such that, if
T_{0}\geq T_{1}
, then

\forall\,t\in[T_{0},T[,\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\big(2\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}+T_{0}^{-1/6}C_{\mathrm{boot}}\big)\varepsilon.

Proof.

We apply the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
to
(g-\mathbf{g})_{\alpha}
, for any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
. In view of the previous Lemma
4.10
, (
BA3
), and (
H

f

), one can estimate
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(g_{\alpha}-\mathbf{g}_{\alpha})
by applying Proposition
3.8
and Corollary
4.3
. Assuming without loss of generality that
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}\geq\mathbf{\Lambda}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](\tau)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](T_{0})+p\!\int_{T_{0}}^{\tau}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big(C_{0}[N,p,\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}]\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+\mathfrak{C}[N,p,\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}]C_{\mathrm{boot}}\varepsilon\Big)\mathrm{d}t,

for all
T_{0}\leq\tau<T
. As
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}
does not depend on
T_{0}
, the Grönwall inequality yields the result.

Let us now show that, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,x)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N-1}\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi[f-\mathbf{f}](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g-\mathbf{g}\big](t)+\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t).

(47)

For the first inequality, we use (
25
) and
\langle v\rangle^{2}|\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}|(t,v)\leq(t+|tv|)^{2}|\nabla_{x}\phi[f]|(t,tv)
. For the second one, we use the elliptic estimates of Corollary
2.3
, together with Proposition
4.5
. We then deduce that there exists
T_{2}[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]\geq 0
such that, if
T_{0}\geq T_{2}
, then

\forall\,T_{0}\leq t<T,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g-\mathbf{g}\big](t).

(48)

Now, we claim that there exists
C[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
, such that

\big(\phi[f-\mathbf{f}],\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big([T_{0},T[,C_{\mathrm{new}}\varepsilon\big),\qquad\qquad C_{\mathrm{new}}\coloneqq C\big(2\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}+T_{0}^{-1/6}C_{\mathrm{boot}}\big).

For this, we follow the analysis carried out in the proof of Corollary
3.3
. First, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N-1}\big[g(t,x,v)-\mathbf{g}\big(t,x+\lambda\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[g-\mathbf{g}](t)+\log^{2N+6}\langle t+1\rangle\mathcal{E}_{N-1}\big[\phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}\big](t)

(49)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(2\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}+T_{0}^{-1/6}C_{\mathrm{boot}}\big)\varepsilon\log^{2N+6}\langle t+1\rangle.

(50)

The first inequality is exactly (
41
), while the second follows from Lemma
4.11
and (
48
). To establish (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
) and (

Φ

, bound
), we combine (
47
)–(
50
) with Lemma
4.11
. To control
\partial_{t}\Phi_{f-\mathbf{f}}
, we apply Lemma
2.16
together with (
50
). Finally, (

L
∞

, sharp
)–(

L
p

, sharp
) follow from (
26
) and (

ϕ

,

L
∞

, weak
)–(

ϕ

,

L
p

, weak
).

To conclude the proof of global existence, it remains to improve (
BA3
). We deal with the time interval
[0,T_{0}[
by choosing
C_{\mathrm{boot}}\geq 2\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}
. For
[T_{0},T[
, we have
C_{\mathrm{new}}\leq 2C_{\mathrm{boot}}/3
, provided that
T_{0}
and
C_{\mathrm{boot}}
are chosen such that

T_{0}\geq\max(T_{1},T_{2}),\qquad\qquad CT_{0}^{-1/6}\leq\frac{1}{3},\qquad\qquad C_{\mathrm{boot}}\geq 6C\mathbf{C}_{T_{0}}.

Finally, let us note that if
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f](0)<+\infty
, higher moments can be propagated by combining the energy inequality of Proposition
2.22
with Proposition
3.8
and Corollary
4.3
.

4.3.3 Modified scattering

We first prove that
g
converges.

Proposition 4.12
.

There exists two functions
f_{\infty},\,\mathbf{f}_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that,

\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]+\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Moreover,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}]\leq\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{top}}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f](0)
,
\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[f_{\infty}-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}]\leq\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{Lip}}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f-\mathbf{f}](0)
, and

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\big]+\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-\mathbf{f}_{\infty}\big]=0.

Proof.

The existence of
f_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{f}_{\infty}
, as well as the corresponding convergence results, follows from Corollary
3.15
. The bounds for
f_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{f}_{\infty}
follow from
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\mathbf{g}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda}
and the energy bounds in Proposition
4.8
.

To conclude this section, let us show that
G(t,\cdot,\cdot)\to f_{\infty}
in
L^{p}_{x,v}
as
t\to+\infty
, where

G(t,x,v)\coloneqq f\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)=g\big(t,x+\lambda\big(\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}](v)-\Phi_{f}(t,v)\big)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

For this, we use the mean value theorem in
x
,
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g](t)<+\infty
, and
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}]-\Phi_{f}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1/2}
, which follows from Corollary
2.17
. Finally, arguing exactly as for the small data solutions in Section
3.5
, one can prove that the convergences in Proposition
4.12
remain valid with
g
replaced by
G
.

5
Continuity of the wave operator

The goal of this section is to complete the proof of Theorem
1.6
. Note first that by the time-reversibility of the Vlasov-Poisson system
2
2

2

The function
f(t,x,v)
is a solution to (
VP
) if and only if the same holds for
f(-t,x,-v)
.
, it suffices to study
\mathscr{W}_{+}
. In view of the results in Section
4.2
, as well as Propositions
4.8
,
4.12
, and
A.2
, it only remains to show that
\mathscr{W}_{+}\colon\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\to\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)
is bicontinous for the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
and
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
be such that
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
. The inverse modified scattering operator
\mathscr{W}_{+}^{-1}\colon\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)\to\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}
is continuous with respect to the norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[\cdot]
.

Proof.

For
(f_{0}^{1},f_{0}^{2})\in\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)^{2}
, we denote, for any
i\in\{1,2\}
,
f_{\infty}^{i}\coloneqq\mathscr{W}_{+}^{-1}(f_{0}^{i})
, and let
f^{i}
be the global solution to (
VP
) arising from the initial data
f_{0}^{i}
. We also set

g^{i}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f^{i}\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}^{i}]\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

We fix
f_{0}^{1}\in\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)
, and we consider
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
such that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{1}](t)=\mathbf{\Lambda}
. In what follows, the constant
C\geq\mathbf{\Lambda}
may vary from line to line, but depends only on
(N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda})
. Let
\varepsilon>0
and
f_{0}^{2}\in\mathscr{W}_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}\big)
be such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{0}^{1}-f_{0}^{2}]\leq\delta
, where
0<\delta\leq\varepsilon
is a constant to be chosen later. If
\varepsilon
is small enough, then

•

\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[f_{\infty}^{1}-f^{2}_{\infty}]\leq C\varepsilon
and
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{\infty}^{2}]\leq C
by Proposition
4.12
,

•

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{2}](t)\leq C
for all
t\geq 0
according to Proposition
4.7
,

•

\big(\phi[f^{i}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f_{\infty}^{i}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(\mathbb{R}_{+},C)
, for any
i\in\{1,2\}
, by Corollary
3.5
.

Note now that we have, for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}^{1}-f_{\infty}^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[f^{1}_{\infty}-g^{1}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big]+\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[g^{1}-g^{2}\big](t)+\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[g^{2}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f^{2}_{\infty}\big].

Let us now prove that there exists
T[N,p,n,\mathbf{\Lambda},\varepsilon]>0
such that, for all
t\geq T
,

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[f^{1}_{\infty}-g^{1}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\big]\leq\varepsilon,\qquad\quad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[g^{2}(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f^{2}_{\infty}\big]\leq 2\varepsilon.

For this, we need to refine Corollary
3.15
. In fact, the result follows from a slight adaptation of the last step of its proof. More precisely, we need to show that the constant
C
in (
30
) can be chosen independently of
g^{2}
whenever

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{1}-g^{2}](0)=\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f^{1}_{0}-f^{2}_{0}]\leq\varepsilon.

This follows from the estimate

\big\|g^{2}_{\alpha}(0,\cdot,\cdot)-h\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\leq\big\|g^{1}_{\alpha}(0,\cdot,\cdot)-h\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{1}-g^{2}](0).

We conclude the proof by applying Cauchy stability. There exists
\delta[N,p,n,\mathbf{\Lambda},T,\varepsilon]>0
such that, if
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f^{1}_{0}-f_{0}^{2}]\leq\delta
, then
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{1}-g^{2}](T)\leq\varepsilon
.

We now show that
\mathscr{W}_{+}
is continuous. In other words, we need to establish Cauchy stability for (
VP
) with asymptotic data. Fix
N\geq 2p_{\infty}
and
n=(n_{x},n_{v})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with
n_{x},\,n_{v}\geq 7
. Let
(f^{k}_{\infty})_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence in
\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}
, and assume that there exists
f_{\infty}^{\infty}\in\mathcal{O}_{+}^{N,n}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
. Let, for
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\cup\{\infty\}
,
f_{0}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{W}_{+}(f_{\infty}^{k})
,
f^{k}
be the global solution to (
VP
) arising from the initial data
f_{0}^{k}
, and

g^{k}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f^{k}\big(t,x+tv+\lambda\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}\big](v)\log\langle t\rangle,v\big).

In view of the assumptions and Proposition
3.18
, there exists
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
such that, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\cup\{\infty\}
,

\forall\,t\geq 0,\quad\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{k}](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[f^{k}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}\big]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big).

(decay,
\phi[f^{k}]
)

The strategy is the following. By Corollary
4.6
, we already have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g^{k}-g^{\infty}](t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f^{\infty}_{\infty}]
, so we only need to show convergence at top order. For any
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|=N-1
, we write

g^{k}_{\alpha}=M^{k}_{\alpha}+R^{k}_{\alpha},\qquad\qquad g_{\alpha}^{k}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\langle x\rangle^{n_{x}+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{n_{v}+N-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g^{k}(t,x,v),

where

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}\big(M^{k}_{\alpha}\big)=\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}\big(g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}\big(R^{k}_{\alpha}\big)=\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}\big(g^{k}_{\alpha}\big)-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}\big(g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle M_{\alpha}^{k}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}\big[f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle R^{k}_{\alpha}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}\big[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that
M^{\infty}_{\alpha}=g^{\infty}_{\alpha}
and
R^{\infty}_{\alpha}=0
. Moreover, since the source terms decays sufficiently fast in time according to Proposition
3.8
, a slight adaptation of Proposition
3.16
shows that
M^{k}_{\alpha}
and
R^{k}_{\alpha}
are well-defined.

For convenience, we define the norms

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{1}[h](t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\|h(t,x,v)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\big\|\nabla_{v}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\big\|\langle x\rangle\langle v\rangle^{-1}\nabla_{x}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{2}[h](t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbf{E}_{1}[h](t)+\big\|\nabla_{v}^{2}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\big\|\langle x\rangle\langle v\rangle^{-1}\nabla_{x}\nabla_{v}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\big\|\langle x\rangle^{2}\langle v\rangle^{-2}\nabla^{2}_{x}h(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}.

Then,
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[f_{0}^{k}-f_{0}^{\infty}]\to 0
, as
k\to+\infty
, would follow if we could show, for any
|\alpha|=N-1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M^{k}_{\alpha}-g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big](t)=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[R^{k}_{\alpha}\big](t)=0.

This is the purpose of the next two results, which will conclude the proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
|\alpha|=N-1
, we have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M^{k}_{\alpha}-g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big](t)\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
.

Proof.

Fix
|\alpha|=N-1
. Let
(\rho_{\epsilon})_{0<\epsilon\leq 1}
be a mollifier, and
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
be a cutoff function such that
\chi(s)=1
for
s\leq 1
, and
\chi(s)=0
for
s\geq 2
. We define
F_{\infty}\coloneqq\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}(g^{\infty}_{\alpha})
, and

F_{j}\coloneqq\chi\big(|x,v|/j\big)\cdot F_{\infty}\ast_{x,v}\rho_{1/j},\qquad\qquad m_{j}\coloneqq\chi\big(|x,v|/j\big)\cdot\big[f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}\ast_{x,v}\rho_{1/j},\qquad\qquad m_{\infty}=\big[f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}.

Then, we define, for
j,\,k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\cup\{\infty\}
,
M^{k}_{\alpha,j}
as the unique solution to

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}\big(M^{k}_{\alpha,j}\big)=F_{j},\qquad\qquad M_{\alpha,j}^{k}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}m_{j},

so that
M_{\alpha,\infty}^{k}=M_{\alpha}^{k}
. Next, note that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[m_{j}-m_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[m_{\infty}\big]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big],\qquad\qquad\mathbf{E}_{2}\big[m_{j}\big]\leq C_{j},

for some constant
C_{j}>0
. Let us also prove that, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[F_{j}-F_{\infty}\big](t)\lesssim\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[F_{\infty}\big](t)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-4/3},\qquad\qquad\mathbf{E}_{2}\big[F_{j}\big](t)\leq C_{j}\langle t\rangle^{-4/3}.

(51)

For the first inequality, we use in particular Proposition
3.8
and (
27
) to control
F_{\infty}
. For the second one, we use the compact support of
F_{j}
to handle the
\langle x\rangle
-weights associated with the second-order derivatives. By the dominated convergence theorem, we then get

\lim_{j\to+\infty}\int_{t=0}^{+\infty}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[F_{j}-F_{\infty}\big](t)\mathrm{d}t=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{j\to+\infty}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[m_{j}-m_{\infty}\big]=0.

(52)

In view of (
decay,

ϕ

[

f
k

]

) and (
51
), the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
, together with Proposition
3.8
, yields

\sup_{1\leq k,\,j\leq\infty}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M^{k}_{\alpha,j}\big](t)\leq C[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}],\qquad\qquad\sup_{1\leq k\leq\infty}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\mathbf{E}_{2}\big[M^{k}_{\alpha,j}\big](t)\leq C_{j}[N,n,p,\mathbf{\Lambda},j].

Write next, for
t\geq 0
, that

\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha}^{\infty}\big](t)\leq\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{k}\big](t)+\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha,j}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{\infty}\big](t)+\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha,j}^{\infty}-M_{\alpha}^{\infty}\big](t).

Let
\varepsilon>0
. We shall prove that for all sufficiently large
j

\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\cup\{\infty\},\qquad\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{k}\big](t)\leq\varepsilon.

For this, note that
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}(M^{k}_{\alpha}-M^{k}_{\alpha,j})=F_{\infty}-F_{j}
. Then, the energy estimate of Proposition

yields

\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{k}\big](\tau)\lesssim\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[m_{j}-m_{\infty}\big]+\int_{t=\tau}^{+\infty}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[F_{j}-F_{\infty}\big](t)+\sum_{|\kappa|=1}\big\|\big[\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]},\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big]\big(M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{k}\big)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t.

The last term in the integral, on the right hand side, can be bounded by
\langle t\rangle^{-4/3}\mathbf{E}_{1}[M_{\alpha}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{k}]
using Proposition

and (
decay,

ϕ

[

f

). Then, it remains to use the Grönwall inequality, together with (

).

Next, we observe that

\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M_{\alpha,j}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{\infty}\big](\tau)\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{t=\tau}^{+\infty}\Big\|\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}\Big(\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big(M_{\alpha,j}^{k}-M_{\alpha,j}^{\infty}\big)\Big)(t,x,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}t.

In view of the assumptions on

, and Corollary

, we have

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[g^{k}-g^{\infty}\big]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big],\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[f^{k}-f^{\infty}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}-f^{\infty}_{\infty}\big]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},C\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]\big),

where

. We can then apply Proposition

and Corollary
3
3

3

Strictly speaking, since

and

), Corollary

4.3
, together with (

[

) and

, to obtain

Hence, by choosing first

.

We now give the key ideas to show that the remainder

goes to

, as

.

Lemma 5.4
.

For any
|\alpha|=N-1
, we have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[R^{k}_{\alpha}\big](t)\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
.

Proof.

We apply first Proposition
2.22
. It allows to bound
\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[R^{k}_{\alpha}\big](\tau)
by

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]+p\sum_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{t=\tau}^{+\infty}\bigg\|\bigg[\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]},\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\bigg]\big(R^{k}_{\alpha}\big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\!+\bigg\|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}(g^{k}_{\alpha})-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}(g_{\alpha}^{\infty})\big]\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t.

By applying once again Proposition
3.8
, we control the first term in the integral by
\langle t\rangle^{-4/3}\mathbf{E}_{1}[R^{k}_{\alpha}]
. For the second one, we claim that for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}(g^{k}_{\alpha})-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}(g_{\alpha}^{\infty})\big](t,x,v)\bigg\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[f_{\infty}^{k}-f^{\infty}_{\infty}\big]+\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M^{k}_{\alpha}-g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big](t)+\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[R^{k}_{\alpha}\big](t)+\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[g^{\infty}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}\big](t)\Big),

where
\widetilde{g}^{\infty}(t,x,v)\coloneqq g^{\infty}\big(t,x+\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)
. For this, the main steps are the following.

•

We compute
\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{-|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}(g^{k}_{\alpha})-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{\infty}]}(g_{\alpha}^{\infty})\big]
by applying Corollary
2.20
. Then, we estimate it by performing an analysis similar to the one carried out in Lemma
4.1
and Corollary
4.2
, when we controlled
\big[\,\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f^{k}]}(g^{k}-g^{\infty})\big]_{\alpha}
. The main difference here is that
g^{\infty}_{\alpha}
is not differentiated more than the other factors.

•

Then, in the error terms, apart from the top order derivatives of
\phi[f^{k}-f^{\infty}]
and
g^{k}-g^{\infty}
, we control all the factors using, for
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\cup\{\infty\}
large enough,

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{k}]\leq\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}[g^{k}-g^{\infty}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big],\qquad\mathcal{E}_{N}\big[\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\big]\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N}\big[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big],

and that the force fields satisfy

\big(\phi[f^{k}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big),\qquad\big(\phi[f^{k}-f^{\infty}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N-1}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},C\mathbb{E}_{N-1}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\big),

for some constant
C[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
. To deal with the top order derivatives, we observe that for any
|\beta|=N
, there exists
|\kappa|=1
and
|\alpha|=N-1
such that

\Big|\big[g^{k}-g^{\infty}\big]_{\beta}\Big|\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}\Big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[g^{k}-g^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}\Big|+\Big|\big[g^{k}-g^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}\Big|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}{\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{x}|}}\Big(\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big(M^{k}_{\alpha}-g^{\infty}_{\alpha}\big)\big|+\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}R^{k}_{\alpha}\big|\Big)+\Big|\big[g^{k}-g^{\infty}\big]_{\alpha}\Big|,

where the last term is lower order. In particular, it yields

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g^{k}-g^{\infty}](t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{N-1}^{n}\big[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}\big]+\sum_{|\gamma|=N-1}\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[M^{k}_{\gamma}-M^{\infty}_{\gamma}\big](t)+\mathbf{E}_{1}\big[R^{k}_{\gamma}\big](t).

For the force field, introducing
\widetilde{g}^{\infty}(t,x,v)\coloneqq g^{\infty}\big(t,x+\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\log\langle t\rangle,v\big)
, Corollary
3.5
yields

\big(\phi[f^{k}-f^{\infty}],\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},C(\mathbb{E}_{N}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f_{\infty}^{\infty}]+\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{k}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}])\big),\qquad\qquad C[N,p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0.

It remains to note
\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{k}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{k}-g^{\infty}]+\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{\infty}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}]
.

We conclude by applying the Grönwall inequality, together with the previous Lemma
5.3
and

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\int_{t=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{\infty}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}](t)\mathrm{d}t=0.

This last property follows from the dominated convergence theorem since

•

\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{\infty}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}](t)\lesssim\log^{2N+7}\langle t+1\rangle
, by Lemma
2.6
applied with
f_{\circ}=\widetilde{g}^{\infty}
,
g=g^{\infty}
and
\Phi=-\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f^{\infty}_{\infty}]
,

•

\mathbb{E}_{N}[g^{\infty}-\widetilde{g}^{\infty}](t)\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
for almost all
t\geq 0
. This follows from the two estimates established in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, that
\mathcal{E}_{N}\big[\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}[f^{k}_{\infty}-f^{\infty}_{\infty}]\big]\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
, and an approximation argument to handle the top order derivatives.

Appendix A
A top order energy estimate and applications

We begin with an energy estimate which, although not optimal with respect to the logarithmic growth, is sufficient for our purposes. In particular, it will allow us to control higher-order derivatives of spherically symmetric solutions to (
VP
) in the repulsive case.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
N_{0}\geq 2p_{\infty}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda},\,a\geq 0
, and
f\colon[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a solution to (
VP
) such that

•

\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\big|\nabla_{x}\phi[f]\big|(t,x)\leq 2\mathbf{\Lambda}
for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
,

•

\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{1+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\leq 2\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle
for all
t\in[0,T]
and any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-1
,

Assume
\mathbb{E}_{N_{0}}[f](0)<+\infty
and let
f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+tv,v)
. Then, there exists
\mathbf{C}[N_{0},p,\mathbf{\Lambda}]>0
such that,

\forall\,t\in[0,T],\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}\mathbb{E}_{N_{0}}[f](0)\log^{7+(a+15+2N_{0})N_{0}}\langle t+1\rangle.

Similarly, if
\mathbb{E}_{N_{0}}[f](T)<+\infty
, we have

\forall\,t\in[0,T],\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](t)\leq\mathbf{C}\mathbb{E}_{N_{0}}[f](T)\log^{7+(a+15+2N_{0})N_{0}}\bigg(1+\frac{\langle T\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\bigg).

Proof.

Since the two cases are similar, we only treat the forward-in-time estimate. We will apply the results of Section
2
with
\Phi=0
, so that
g=f_{\circ}
. To avoid any confusion, let

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}\coloneqq\partial_{t}+\lambda t\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,x+tv)\cdot\nabla_{v},

(53)

which coincides with
\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}
when
\Phi=0
. It will be convenient to introduce the function

\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x,v)\coloneqq x+\psi(t,x,v),\qquad\qquad\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(\psi)=-\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(x),\quad\psi(0,\cdot,\cdot)=0,

as well as the notation

f_{\circ}^{\alpha}(t,x,v)\coloneqq\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle^{7+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{7+N_{0}-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v),\qquad\qquad|\alpha|\leq N_{0}.

In particular,
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
. To propagate and exploit moments in
v
and
\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}
, observe that

\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}\big(\langle v\rangle\big)\big|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2},\qquad\qquad\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(x)\big|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1}.

(54)

Duhamel’s principle then allows to estimate
\psi
, which yields

\langle x\rangle\lesssim\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle\log\langle t+1\rangle,\qquad\qquad\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle\lesssim\langle x\rangle\log\langle t+1\rangle.

(55)

We will also use that, for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\langle t\rangle^{2-\frac{3}{p}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{p}+|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\mathbf{\Lambda}\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle,\qquad\qquad 1\leq|\kappa|\leq N_{0}-p_{\infty},

(56)

which follows from the assumptions and Proposition
2.1
. Let us show that, for any
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big\|f_{\circ}^{\alpha}(\tau,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}-\big\|f_{\circ}^{\alpha}(0,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim\int_{t=0}^{t}\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}\big(f_{\circ}^{\alpha}\big)(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq N_{0}}\int_{t=0}^{\tau}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N_{0},\,|\kappa_{v}|<|\alpha_{v}|}\int_{t=0}^{\tau}\frac{\log^{a+14+|\alpha_{x}|+|\kappa_{x}|}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\kappa}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\mathrm{d}t.

(57)

The first inequality follows from Proposition
2.22
. One cannot directly apply the commutation formula of Corollary
2.21
since it is stated with the weight
\langle x\rangle
rather than
\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle
. We deal with this issue using (
55
), which gives rise to the
\log^{14+|\alpha_{x}|+|\kappa_{x}|}\langle t+1\rangle
losses in (
57
). Then, using (
54
),
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi[f]}(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
and Corollary
2.21
, applied with
\Phi=0
, we reduce the proof of (
57
) to showing that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\|\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle}t^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,x+tv)\cdot f_{\circ}^{\beta}\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},\qquad\gamma+\beta_{v}=\alpha_{v},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\langle t+|x+tv|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,x+tv)\cdot f_{\circ}^{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

where
1\leq|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
and
\big(|\gamma|-1\big)+|\beta|\leq N_{0}
. Observe for this that, as
t\langle v\rangle\leq t+t|v|\leq t+|x|+|x-tv|
, we have

\forall\,(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad\frac{\langle v\rangle t}{\langle x\rangle\,\langle t+|x+tv|\rangle^{1+\frac{3}{p}}\langle t\rangle^{1-\frac{3}{p}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle}.

(58)

Assume first that
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-p_{\infty}
. Then, we estimate pointwise the force field using the assumptions and (
56
). It yields, using also (
58
) for
\mathcal{I}_{1}
, that

\mathcal{I}_{1}\lesssim\frac{\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{I}_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Otherwise, we have
|\gamma|\geq N_{0}+1-p_{\infty}
and
|\beta|\leq p_{\infty}
. Using again (
58
), and the change of variables
y(x)=x+tv
, we get

\mathcal{I}_{1}+\langle t\rangle\cdot\mathcal{I}_{2}\lesssim\langle t\rangle\Big\|\langle t+|y|\rangle^{|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f\big](t,y)\cdot f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,y-tv,v)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\log^{a}\langle t+1\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{1-\frac{3}{p}}}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,y-tv,v)\big\|_{L^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\big)}.

If
t\leq 1
, we use the Sobolev embedding for Banach-valued functions
W^{p_{\infty},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))
. If
t\geq 1
, we perform the change of variables
y^{\prime}(v)=y-tv
, and we use a Sobolev embedding in the velocity variable. It yields

\big\|f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,y-tv,v)\big\|_{L^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y},L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\big)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{p}}}\sum_{|\kappa|\leq p_{\infty}}\big\|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}f_{\circ}^{\beta}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{p}}}\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+p_{\infty},\,\kappa_{v}\leq\beta_{v}+p_{\infty}}\big\|f_{\circ}^{\kappa}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}.

For
\mathcal{I}_{1}
, since
|\gamma|\geq N_{0}+1-p_{\infty}
,
|\gamma|+|\beta_{v}|=|\alpha_{v}|
, and
N_{0}\geq 2p_{\infty}
, we have
|\beta_{v}|+p_{\infty}<|\alpha_{v}|
. This concludes the proof of (
57
). Using
14+|\alpha_{x}|+|\kappa_{x}|\leq 14+2N_{0}
, and a triangular Grönwall inequality, we obtain

\big\|\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle^{7+|\alpha_{x}|}\langle v\rangle^{N_{0}+7-|\alpha_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq\mathbf{C}\mathbb{E}_{N_{0}}[f](0)\log^{(a+15+2N_{0})|\alpha_{v}|}\langle t+1\rangle.

The result then follows by bounding
\langle\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{mod}}\rangle
using (
55
).

A.1  Boundedness of spherically symmetric solutions for the repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system

We assume here that
\lambda=-1
. The purpose of this section is to show the following result.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
N\geq 1
, and
f_{0}\in C_{c}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R}_{+})
be a spherically symmetric initial distribution function. Then, the unique classical solution
f
to (
VP
), arising from the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies the following properties.

(
I
)

f\in C^{N}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
is global in time and there exists
K\geq 0
such that

\mathrm{supp}\,f_{\circ}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\subset\big\{(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\;\big|\;|x|\leq K\log\langle t+1\rangle,\;|v|\leq K\big\},\qquad f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+tv,v).

(
II
)

For all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
, we have
|\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,x)\big|\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}
.

(
III
)

There exists
q\geq 0
such that, for any
|\alpha|,\,|\gamma|\leq N
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, there holds

\big\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}f_{\circ}(t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\log^{q}\langle t+1\rangle,\qquad\langle t\rangle^{|\gamma|+3-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\log^{q}\langle t+1\rangle.

(
IV
)

Let
\Phi_{f}(t,v)\coloneqq t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,tv)
and
g(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+tv+\lambda\Phi_{f}(t,v)\log\langle t\rangle,v)
. There exists
\mathbf{\Lambda}\geq 0
such that, for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{n}[g](t)\leq\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad\qquad\big(\phi[f],\Phi_{f}\big)\in\mathcal{W}^{N}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbf{\Lambda}\big).

The global existence and
C^{N}
regularity of
f
follow from
[
26
,
20
]
. According to
[
21
, Lemmata 3.1 and 5.1]
,

|\nabla_{x}\phi[f](t,x)\big|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2},\qquad\qquad|\rho[f]|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3}.

It yields
|\mathbf{T}_{\phi[f]}(|v|)|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}
and
|\mathbf{T}(|x-tv|)|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1}
, which implies
(I)
. Hence, there exists
K_{0}
such that
\rho[f](t,x)=0
for
|x|\geq K_{0}\langle t\rangle
. As a result, we get
(II)
from Proposition
2.2
. To show
(III)
, we will require

\forall\,(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad\big|\nabla_{x}f_{\circ}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim 1,\qquad\big|\nabla_{v}f_{\circ}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\log\langle t+1\rangle,

(59)

which have been proved in from
[
21
, Lemma 8.1]
, and in the proof of
[
21
, Lemma 9.3]
. To derive
L^{p}
-estimates for the force field, we will use the next result.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
|\alpha|\leq N
. Then, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\langle t\rangle^{3\frac{p-1}{p}+|\alpha|}\big\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\big\|\langle x\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{4}\big[|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}|+|\partial_{v}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}|\big](t,x,v)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Proof.

Note, using first an elliptic estimate from Proposition
2.2
and then the identity (
10
), that

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle^{|\alpha|}\big\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{|\alpha|}\big\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\rho[f](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big[|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}|+|\partial_{v}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}|\big](t,x-tv,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

It remains to apply the Hölder inequality, and to note

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-tv\rangle^{4}}\leq\frac{2}{t^{3}},\qquad\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle v\rangle^{4}}\leq 2.

Then, in view of (
59
), the support of
f_{\circ}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
, and the previous Lemma
A.3
,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\sum_{|\gamma|\leq q}\langle t\rangle^{|\gamma|+3-\frac{3}{p}}\big\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi[\partial_{x}^{\gamma}f](t,x)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\log^{a_{q}}\langle t+1\rangle,

(60)

holds for
q=1
, with
a_{1}=5+3/p
.

Let us first show
(III)
for
p>3
, so that
p_{\infty}=1
. Then, by
(II)
and (
60
), the assumptions of Proposition
A.1
are satisfied for
N_{0}=2p_{\infty}
. We then obtain
(III)
by iterating Proposition
A.1
and Lemma
A.3
.

We now consider the case
3/2<p\leq 3
, so that
p_{\infty}=2
. Using
(III)
for
p=7
, together with the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,7}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, we control
\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}f_{\circ}
pointwise for any
|\alpha|\leq N-1
. Then, by applying Lemma
A.3
, we get that the assumptions of Proposition
A.1
are satisfied for
N_{0}=N-1
, allowing to derive
(III)
.

We finally prove
(IV)
. Applying Corollary
3.3
with
\Phi=0
, we get
(\phi[f],\Phi_{f})\in\mathcal{W}^{N}(\mathbb{R}_{+},C\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}])
for some constant
C\geq 0
. Note further that
\mathbb{E}_{N}[f_{\circ}](t)\lesssim\log^{q+N+7}\langle t+1\rangle
by support considerations and
(III)
. Then, the energy estimate of Proposition
2.22
, together with Propostion
3.8
, yields to
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{(n_{x},n_{v})}[g](t)<+\infty
. We conclude by applying Corollary
3.3
with
\Phi=\Phi_{f}
and
a=q+N+7
.
